## 0 · The year in one paragraph

**First year with JK; the 2024 return was prepared by a Chicago firm.** He is a shareholder in
**three trucking S corporations** and holds **no W-2, no 1099 and no K-1** for 2025 — he fell out
with his business partner and was locked out of the companies. **Julia decided on 2026-09-21 to
prepare and file without the K-1s and amend if they arrive.** The single largest figure on the
return is not something he earned this year: it is a **net operating loss carried forward from
2024**. 🔵 **UPDATED 2026-09-22 — LILIAN RULED THE PRIOR RETURN IS ACCEPTED, NOT AUDITED**
*("hay que asumir que todo lo que hay ahí es correcto… no vamos a inventar declaraciones hechas por
otros contadores")*. ✅ **That settles what had been the biggest open item on this file: the NOL is
used AS REPORTED, and his opening 2025 basis is ZERO** (§2F-i). ⛔ **Two things her ruling does NOT
reach, and they stay with Julia: the two forms that were REQUIRED on the 2024 return and are missing
(Form 7203, Form 461), and her own confirmation as SIGNER that 2025 may be prepared on the accepted
figures.** ⚠️ *Neither stops the work.*

🔵 **UPDATED 2026-09-21 — Julia settled the shape of the return:** file without the K-1s, assume
**no Marketplace coverage**, and report the **gross** sum from Midwest Expedited with **no expenses**
on his 1040, because the expenses belong on the corporation's 1120-S. 🟠 **That last instruction IMPLIES the business is the CORPORATION'S, and therefore that there is no
Schedule C — but ⛔ that is a READING of her instruction, not a decision she gave, and it is not
recorded as one.** **Put it to her in terms before filing** (§4A). ⚠️ *An earlier version of this
paragraph marked it ✅ settled; struck — the file's own rule is that a permission or a determination
is widened by asking, never by reasoning.*
⚠️ **What it does NOT settle is the CHARACTER of the money** — an allocated K-1 share, or a
distribution. **§4A works both routes and recommends one.**

🔴 **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23 — READ THIS BEFORE THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE, WHICH IS NO LONGER THE PLAN.**
**Julia reversed the shape of the return:** the 200,053 goes on a **SCHEDULE C** with the client's
own expenses, **zero salary and zero distributions**, **nothing in the K-1 input**, and a **Form 8082
for EACH of the three corporations** reporting nothing from any of them.
⛔ **So the paragraph above is backwards on two counts now: the expenses ARE deducted on his 1040,
and there IS a Schedule C.** ✅ **The reading it flagged — "is there a Schedule C?" — was put to her
and came back the other way.**
🔑 **§4E is the decision, what it costs, and the preparer's objection; §4F is the forms line by
line.** ⚠️ **§4 decision 3, §3A, §3B, §4A, §4B, §4C and §4D describe the ROUTE NOT TAKEN**, and so do
**§3C's line-1 block, §5A and §9A** — each carries its own banner saying what in it is dead and what
survives, **and none of them is what gets typed.** ⚠️ **Treat that as the list of what IS marked,
not as a guarantee nothing else is stale** — **eight** stale passages have been found across four
review rounds, each one behind the previous round's assurance.
🔑 **SO HERE IS A TEST YOU CAN APPLY YOURSELF, because the marking was done by SECTION while the
staleness lives in SENTENCES:** ⛔ **a passage that says *box 1*, *box 16D*, *Schedule E*, *Form
7203*, *distribution*, *allocated share* or *decision 3* in the PRESENT TENSE, and is not under a
banner, is stale.** ✅ **§4E and §4F are the authority** — if something disagrees with them, they
win, and that disposes in one glance of the over-triggers this list will produce inside those two
sections. ⚠️ **§8 is the one place it matters most**, because it is the live to-do list and the
client question list is assembled from it.
ⓘ *The last three terms were added after the first four were tested against the four stale §8 items
and **caught only two.** The other two — 0a and item 2 — were stale in CONCEPT, not in form address,
and they were the two that mattered more: 0a would have told the signer the return knowingly

## 2 · The 2024 carryforward — what the prior return actually says

**This section is the reason this file was opened.** Read from the 2024 filed return on
2026-09-21, at Lilian's request, to establish what carries into 2025.

### 2A · The S-corporation losses (Schedule E, Part II)

| | Company | EIN | 2024 loss, **nonpassive** column |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | **Midwest Expedited Corp** | 47-5147826 | **630,737** |
| B | Midwest Truck Leasing LLC | 87-4220280 | 168 |
| C | AVDM Holdings Inc | 87-4182570 | 168 |
| | **Schedule E line 31 / 32** | | **(631,073)** |

🔑 **Midwest Expedited Corp is 99.95% of the total.** The other two are $168 each — token amounts.
**For every practical purpose this is one company's loss.**

🔗 **And it is the same company that paid him in 2025** (Lilian, 2026-09-21), which is what turns
the basis question below from housekeeping into money.

### 2B · How the loss became the NOL

| | | Source |
|---|---|---|
| W-2 wages | **41,795** | ƒ **derived, not read**: 1040 line 9 − line 5b − Sch 1 line 10. ⚠️ **This assumes 1040 lines 2b, 3b, 4b, 6b and 7 are all zero, and none of them was read.** Form 172 line 7 (nonbusiness income) = 31,421 = the pension exactly, which argues interest, dividends and IRA are nil — **but 1040 line 7, capital gains, is NOT settled by that, and it is Form 461 line 3** (§2E) |
| Pension / annuity, taxable | **31,421** | 📖 1040 line 5b — and it equals Form 172 line 7, nonbusiness income ✅ |
| Schedule C (the spouse's) | **131** | 📖 Sch 1 line 3 |
| Form 4797, sale of business property | **49,000** | 📖 Sch 1 line 4 |
| S-corporation loss | **(631,073)** | 📖 Schedule E line 32 = Sch 1 line 5 |
| **Total income (1040 line 9)** | **(508,726)** | 📖 ✅ ties: 41,795 + 31,421 + 131 + 49,000 − 631,073 |
| **Adjusted gross income** | **(508,726)** | 📖 line 11 — **equal to line 9, so there were NO above-the-line adjustments** |
| Standard deduction (MFJ) | (29,200) | 📖 Form 1040 line 12 |
| **Form 172 line 1** | **(537,926)** | 📖 |
| **Form 172 line 24 — the 2024 NOL** | **(537,926)** | 📖 |
| Form 1040 line 15 — taxable income | **0** | 📖 |

**Tie-out: −508,726 − 29,200 = −537,926.** ✅ Exact.

**Form 172 Part I, the other lines:** line 5 = 0 · line 6 (nonbusiness deductions) = **29,200**,
which is the 2024 MFJ standard deduction · line 7 (nonbusiness income) = 31,421 · line 8 = 31,421 ·
lines 9, 10, 13, 14, 15, 22 = **0**. **No add-back adjustments**, which is why line 24 equals line 1.

### 2C · 🔵 What carries to 2025 — and what is certain about it

| | |
|---|---|
| **NOL carried into 2025, as the 2024 return reports it** | **537,926** |
| Consumed in 2024 | **none — and note WHY, because it is definitional, not a finding:** an NOL *generated* in 2024 cannot be consumed in 2024 |
| Form 8582, **prior years' unallowed** column | **276** ⚠️ **This is the amount brought INTO 2024, not the amount carried OUT.** The 2024 unallowed figure was not read. 🟢 **WHICH ACTIVITY PRODUCED IT *IS* NOW ESTABLISHED — 2026-09-24, read off Form 8582 Part VII of the 2024 return:** ⛔ ~~it is not established either~~. **Part VII lists exactly two activities — `MIDWEST TRUCK LEASING LLC` and `AVDM HOLDINGS INC`, at 138 each, to be reported on `E Ln 28B` and `E Ln 28C`.** 🔑 **So the 276 is these two S corporations' own prior-year unallowed loss, not a rental.** ⚠️ **It still sits under *All Other Passive Activities* while §2A puts the S-corp losses in the nonpassive column — that tension is real and is the thing to resolve, but it is no longer a question of WHICH activity.** **Do not carry 276 to 2025 without reading the 2024 8582 worksheets** (§2C-bis) |

✅ **The carryforward EXISTS and it is the full amount.** **This is not the uncertain part.**

🔑 **What the two blank items actually prove — an earlier version of this paper used them loosely:**
- **Schedule 1 line 8a blank** → **no EARLIER NOL was deducted in 2024**, which is evidence there
  was no pre-2024 carryforward.
- **Form 172 line 23** *("NOL deduction for losses from other years")* **= 0** → the same point,
  independently. ⓘ *Derived: line 24 = "Combine lines 1, 9, 17, and 21 through 23" and line 24
  equals line 1 exactly, so lines 17, 21, 22 and 23 are all zero.*
- **Form 172 Part II empty** → no **carryover/carryback** worksheet was computed.
⛔ **None of them proves "the NOL was not used", which was never possible in its year of origin.**

### 2C-bis · 🟠 The Schedule E read — what it proves, and what it does NOT

_(Read 2026-09-21, at Lilian's request. **Rewritten the same day after review**, because the first
version claimed considerably more than the evidence carries.)_

**What was read.** The return carries **Schedule E page 2** (attachment sequence 13, "Page 2"),
holding **Part II** — the three S corps (§2A) — with **Part III** (estates and trusts, lines 35-37),
**Part IV** (REMICs, line 39) and **line 40** (farm rental) all blank, and **line 30** blank.
The S-corp losses sit in the **nonpassive** column because column **(i)** is used rather than **(g)**
(§2A), which is what establishes their character. ⓘ *An earlier version said line 30 was "the
passive-income columns" and that this is why the loss is nonpassive — wrong on both counts: line 30
is **(h) + (k)**, i.e. K-1 income of BOTH kinds, and line 31 mixes both too.*

✅ **WHAT IS PROVEN, and it is extraction-independent:**

> Schedule E line 41 reads *"Total income or (loss). **Combine lines 26, 32, 37, 39, and 40.** Enter
> the result here and on **Schedule 1 (Form 1040), line 5**."* Schedule 1 line 5 is **(631,073)**,
> which is **line 32 alone** — and Part II being in use means line 26's own shortcut to Schedule 1
> is unavailable, so line 5 really does receive line 41.

**Therefore `26 + 37 + 39 + 40 = 0`.** Combined with the extraction, which read **37, 39 and 40 as
blank**, that gives **line 26 = 0**:

🔑 **SCHEDULE E PART I CONTRIBUTED ZERO TO SCHEDULE 1 LINE 5.** ⛔ **That is the whole of the
finding. Three things an earlier version claimed do NOT follow from it:**

- ⛔ **"Page 1 is not in the return."** **Arithmetic cannot prove a page's absence.** It proves a
  sum.
- ⛔ **"The four lines are all zero" as pure arithmetic.** The sum being zero permits them to offset;
  it is the **extraction** that reads three of them blank. The first version said "provable
  arithmetically *rather than* from the extraction" — **it is both, and the extraction half is the
  part that can be wrong.**
- 🔴 **"He reported no rental property in 2024." THIS IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, AND THE RETURN ITSELF
  ARGUES AGAINST IT.** Schedule E line 22 is *"Deductible rental real estate loss **after
  limitation, if any, on Form 8582**"*, line 24 adds only **positive** amounts from line 21, and
  line 25 adds the line 22 losses. **So a rental whose loss is entirely disallowed under §469 gives
  line 26 = 0 with Part I fully populated** — address, fair-rental days, rents, every expense.
  ⚠️ **And this return CONTAINS a Form 8582**, carrying 276 of *"prior years' unallowed losses"*
  under **All Other Passive Activities** — a category that **includes rental real estate without
  active participation**. §2A puts the S-corp losses entirely in the **nonpassive** column, so
  **Part II is not that passive activity's home.** ⛔ **Where it IS reported has not been
  established, and until it is, "no rental in 2024" cannot be asserted.**

🟠 **So the honest statement of what this does for the Minnesota question:** Part I contributed
**zero**, which is *consistent* with the client's 2026-08-17 answer about 2025 and is a point in its
favour — **but it is not independent proof, and it says nothing about years other than 2024**, which
is the only year read (§6). ⚠️ **It also does not establish whether he still owned the house at 31
December 2025**, which is what Minnesota now turns on (client file §5 and its outstanding items — **not** §8 of this paper, which an earlier version wrongly pointed at).

📋 **The open item this creates:** **which activity produced the Form 8582 prior-years' unallowed
losses, and is Schedule E Part I populated?** Both are answered by reading the 2024 Form 8582
worksheets and Schedule E page 1 — **a re-read of a document already within permission**, not a new
request.

ⓘ **A correction for the record:** an earlier sweep in the same session counted page 22 as Schedule
**E**. ⛔ **It is Schedule EIC** — the text search matched *"Schedule E"* inside *"SCHEDULE EIC"*.
Nothing downstream depended on it.

## ✅ 2F-i · THE BASIS ANSWER — **ZERO**, and it comes from ACCEPTING the prior return, not from auditing it

> 🔑 **LILIAN'S RULING, 2026-09-22, and it settles this section:** *"Mi objetivo no es tratar de
> corregir la declaración anterior. Hay que asumir que todo lo que hay ahí es correcto. No vamos a
> inventar declaraciones hechas por otros contadores. Vamos a trabajar en base a lo que tenemos ahora
> y lo que nos entrega el cliente. A menos que sea un error extremadamente grande, [Julia] tendrá que
> decidir, pero vamos a asumir que todo está perfecto."*

**What she ruled, and what this session derived from it — kept apart on purpose.** ⛔ **Her words
above settle ONE thing: the prior return is accepted as correct and is not to be audited.** She also
put the consequence in her own question — *"¿podemos saber que su base en el 7203, a pesar de que no
está incluido, sería cero?"* — and asked whether the logic held. 🔑 **The statutory chain below, and
the limits on it, are this session's work, confirmed to her.** ⚠️ **So it carries a preparer's
ruling on the premise and an assistant's reasoning on the mechanics — not a practitioner's
determination on the law.**

**The premise she accepted:** we hold the corporation's 2024 return, so we know the loss and we know
**630,737** reached his K-1. We hold his 2024 individual return, so we know **the whole of it was
deducted** and became the NOL.

### The chain, and why it closes

| Step | | Authority |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The 2024 return deducted the **full** 630,737 as nonpassive, and **suspended nothing** — no carryover appears anywhere on the pages read | 📖 the filed return |
| 2 | **§1366(d)(1)** caps a loss deduction at *"the basis of your stock and loans from you to the corporation"*. **So the return ASSERTS basis of at least 630,737** | 📎 `i7203.pdf`, *Basis Limitations* |
| 3 | **§1367(a)** adjusts basis **"in the order listed"** — *increases under §1367(a)(1), decreases under §1367(a)(2)*: (1) increased by all income on the K-1 · (2) decreased by **distributions** · (3) decreased by **nondeductible expenses** · (4) decreased by **losses and deductions, last** | 📎 `i7203.pdf` |
| 4 | 🔑 **Each of steps (2)(3)(4) is "decreased (but not below zero)"** — verbatim, three times. So a loss that consumes basis leaves **zero**, not a negative | 📎 `i7203.pdf` |
| 5 | **Debt basis reads zero** — no loans from shareholders on Schedule L, K-1 item I blank, and no other line on the balance sheet carrying one (§2F) | 📖 both returns |
| **=** | **Opening 2025 stock basis: ZERO.** Debt basis: zero **on the reading of the balance sheet** — see the conditional below | |

📎 *Read off the current PDFs on 2026-09-22: `irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i7203.pdf` (Instructions for Form
7203) and `irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i1120ssk.pdf` (Shareholder's Instructions for Schedule K-1). Not from
memory.*

✅ **The obvious challenge, checked and closed: the §1.1367-1(g) election does NOT open a hole.** It
only lets a shareholder *"decrease your basis under (4) prior to decreasing your basis under (3)"* —
losses before nondeductible expenses, with the excess nondeductible amount carried forward. **It
reorders two reductions; it cannot leave residual stock basis.** *(`i7203.pdf`.)*

### ⚠️ The one thing this does NOT establish, stated so nobody over-claims it

**Accepting the return proves basis was *at least* the loss. It does not prove it was not MORE.**
If opening 2024 basis had exceeded 630,737, something would remain.

🔑 **Zero is still the figure to use, for three reasons that require auditing nobody — each bounded
to the search that produced it:**

1. **Nothing in the two returns we have read evidences more.** Form 7203 does not appear in the text
   extracted from either return, and no basis schedule appears on any page read. ⚠️ **That is a
   statement about a 44-page and a 36-page extraction with five partial pages (§6), not about the
   world.**
2. **Substantiating basis is the taxpayer's burden** — ⛔ **and he has not been asked.** The three
   questions only he can answer *(what he paid for the stock, contributions the corporation never
   recorded, a note or a payment under a guarantee)* are on record in the client file and have never
   been put to him.
3. ⛔ **Assuming a leftover would be INVENTING a figure** — which is precisely what her ruling
   forbids. **Zero is the only number a document supports.**

🟢 **The direction of the error, stated precisely.** A basis that is too low **understates what he
may deduct in a future year and can never overstate it** — so on the deduction side it cannot produce
an understated return. ⛔ **That is NOT the same as the "errs toward overpaying" argument §4A rejects
for the box-1 figure, and the two must not be read together**: there, an estimate is written onto an
income line and the return is wrong; here, a carry figure is set at the only documented value.

⚠️ **Two places a zero opening basis BITES, and neither is closed by this ruling:**

- 🔴 **A LOSS YEAR suspends the loss.** *"2025 has income"* is **the firm's chosen reporting figure
  (decision 3), not an established fact** — there is no 2025 K-1, the corporation lost 1,261,474 in
  2024, and §4A keeps open a fifth character in which the money is a **loan draw and not income at
  all**. ⛔ **If the K-1 eventually shows a loss, the loss is suspended AND the distribution becomes a
  §1368(b)(2) gain — both at once.**
- 🟠 **IF he ever had DEBT basis, the 2025 restoration goes to the DEBT first.** Under
  Reg. §1.1367-2(c) *(cited at `i1120ssk.pdf`)* a later year's net increase **restores reduced loan
  basis before stock basis**. **So on that branch 2025's income would rebuild debt basis and leave
  stock basis at zero — and the distribution would be fully taxable.** ⚖️ **Step 5 above is what
  keeps this shut, and step 5 is a reading of a balance sheet at two dates, not a measurement.**

### 🔵 WHAT HER RULING UNBLOCKS — this is the practical gain, and it is large

| Before | After her ruling |
|---|---|
| 🔴 *"The NOL of 537,926 is probably overstated"* — a blocker on the whole return | ✅ **The NOL is used AS REPORTED.** We are not auditing the prior return |
| 🔴 *"Most of the loss should have been suspended"* → a §1366(d)(2) carryforward to track | ✅ **Nothing was suspended, so there is no suspended-loss carryforward.** Simpler, and it matches the filed return |
| 🔴 *2024 may need amending **because the basis figure is wrong*** | ✅ **Not on the basis. No error is established in the figures, and they are accepted** |
| ⛔ **NOT covered by her ruling — two forms that were REQUIRED and are missing** | 🟠 **STILL JULIA'S CALL.** Her words accept the prior return's **figures**. They say nothing about **Form 7203**, required by its own first trigger (§2D), or **Form 461**, owed under the *"$152,500 on any one line"* trigger whatever the arithmetic produced (§2E). ⛔ **Those two questions stay exactly where §2D and §2E left them** |
| 🟠 *Form 7203 line 1 must be reconstructed — separate work* | ✅ **Line 1 is ZERO, with this reasoning written beside it.** The reconstruction is done, and it took one paragraph |

### 🔑 And it resolves the 2025 distribution question — conditionally, not outright

Opening basis **0** → the **2025 income raises it** → **only then** does a distribution reduce it
(§1367's order). **So whatever part of the 2025 money turns out to be a distribution is covered by
the income first, and no §1368(b)(2) capital gain arises** — up to the amount of income reported.
⚠️ *And §1368(c) cannot arise at all: accumulated E&P is zero (§2F-ii).*
⛔ **Both conditions above apply: this holds only while 2025 is an income year and only while debt
basis really is zero.** 🔑 **The 2025 K-1 settles both at a stroke, and nothing else does.**

### ⚖️ THE CONTRARY EVIDENCE — kept in full, and deliberately NOT acted on

⛔ **This is the material her ruling sets aside. It is reproduced here rather than deleted, because
"no error has been established" is the load-bearing claim of this section and it has to be checkable
by whoever reads it next.**

| Component | Evidence | Amount |
|---|---|---|
| Stated capital | Capital stock **1,000** for the whole company; **no additional paid-in capital** | his 50% ≈ **500** |
| Cumulative undistributed income | **AAA at 1 Jan 2024 = 22,513** | his 50% ≈ **11,257** |
| 🔵 **2024 income items — THESE COME FIRST** | M-2 other additions: **gain on disposition of §179 assets 98,000** | his 50% = **49,000** |
| 🔴 **Debt basis — money HE lent the corporation** | Sch L line 19 **blank**; K-1 item I **blank**; line 18 is 3,267 of payroll liability, line 21 blank | **nothing visible** |
| **Visible basis available to absorb the loss** | ≈ 500 + ≈ 11,257 + 49,000 | **≈ 60,750** |

⛔ **An earlier version of this table stopped at ≈ 11,750 and omitted the 98,000 sitting in its own
M-2 table.** **§1366(d) tests basis AFTER the year's income increases and before losses** — step 3
above puts income first — so the opening figure was never the one to compare.

**🔑 AND THAT SAME LINE CLOSES A QUESTION THIS PAPER WAS ABOUT TO SEND JULIA.** **98,000 ÷ 2 = 49,000
— exactly his 2024 Schedule 1 line 4, Form 4797 "other gains" (§2B).** **His Form 4797 gain appears
to be his half of the corporation's own §179-asset disposition.** If so it is attributable to the S
corporation's trade or business, **Form 461 line 10 is zero, and §2E's conditional branch resolves
the no-excess-business-loss way.** ⚠️ **Tested, not asserted: confirm against the §179 disposition
statement attached to the 1120-S.**

**⚠️ WHY ≈ 60,750 IS A FLOOR AND NOT A MEASUREMENT — this is what makes "no error established"
true.** From the 2024 Instructions for Form 1120-S:

> *"On the first day of the corporation's first tax year as an S corporation, **the balance of the
> AAA is zero**."*

🔑 **So AAA never contains what he PAID for his shares.** If he bought stock — from the corporation
or from a co-founder — **that cost is his basis and is invisible on the corporation's return by
construction.** For a 50% holder of a company with 7,642,187 of gross receipts that is not remote.
**Three further asymmetries run the same way:** AAA excludes tax-exempt income while **stock basis
includes it**; AAA is reduced by nondeductible expenses whether or not any shareholder could deduct
them; and *"the AAA may have a negative balance at year end"* while **basis cannot go below zero**.
⚠️ **On reliability:** *"An S corporation without AE&P doesn't need to maintain the AAA"* — **this
corporation has no AE&P** (§2F-ii), so its AAA is kept by recommendation, not requirement.

📌 **THE MAGNITUDE, because her escalation clause asks for it.** *"A menos que sea un error
extremadamente grande, [Julia] tendrá que decidir."* **The gap between the visible ≈ 60,750 and the
630,737 deducted is roughly 570,000 — about 90% of the deduction.** ⛔ **By size it clears any
sensible bar; what it does NOT clear is the evidence bar, because the corporation's return cannot
measure his basis at all.** 🔑 **So the answer given to her test is: LARGE IN SIZE, NOT ESTABLISHED
AS AN ERROR.** 📌 **Carried to Julia once as information, with the magnitude, so she can disagree —
and the return is prepared on the accepted figures meanwhile.** ⛔ **It does not stop the work.**

## ✅ 2F-ii · Four things this closes outright

1. ✅ **Accumulated E&P is ZERO** (Schedule M-2 column (c), both ends of the year). ⛔ **So §1368(c)
   does not apply and no distribution can be a dividend.** **§1368(b) governs — one fewer branch.**
2. ✅ **There were NO distributions in 2024** — M-2 line 7 blank, K-1 box 16 zero. **Both sources
   agree.**
3. ✅ **The 41,795 of W-2 wages §2B DERIVED is CORROBORATED from the other side.** Form 1125-E lists
   him by name at **41,795** of officer compensation *(a per-officer line, not half of the 83,590
   total)*. ⚠️ **"Corroborated", not "confirmed"** — officer compensation on the 1120-S and **W-2 box
   1** are not the same figure for a **>2% shareholder**, whose employer-paid health insurance sits
   in box 1 while the corporation may deduct it elsewhere. **Two independent routes agreeing is
   still worth a great deal.**
   ⓘ *He is recorded as devoting **100% of his time** to the business, on 50% of the stock.*
   🔵 **And a fact §4A should use: officer compensation of 83,590 against SALARIES AND WAGES BLANK**,
   on 7,642,187 of gross receipts. **A trucking company with no non-officer payroll at all** is
   either wholly owner-operated or paying its drivers as contractors — either way it bears directly
   on §4A's warning that reporting the 2025 sum with zero FICA is a position.
4. ✅ **Illinois has a figure, and it is large.** Illinois Schedule B gives his **distributable share
   of base income (loss) = (774,583)**. ⛔ **So Illinois is not speculative for 2024 — the state
   return exists and carries a specific number.** ⚠️ *It is larger than the federal 630,737, which is
   ordinary — Illinois adds back its own items.*

## 🔴 2F-iii · And it OPENS two things, one of them new and large

- 🔴 **LOANS TO SHAREHOLDERS: 994,031 falling to 34,213 during 2024.** Roughly **960,000 came off
  the books in one year and nothing in the return explains how.** ⚠️ **A loan TO a shareholder gives
  him no basis; it is the opposite of the loan that would.**
  🔴 **AND THE RETURN OFFERS NO SOURCE FOR A CASH REPAYMENT OF THAT SIZE:** officer compensation
  **83,590**, salaries and wages **blank**, M-2 distributions **blank**, M-2 other reductions **nil**
  *(the AAA arithmetic ties exactly, so line 5 is zero)*. **On the face of this return the
  shareholders had no corporate money to repay with.**
  🔑 **Which leaves three readings, and an earlier version named only one of them:**
  - repaid from **outside funds**;
  - **written off** — ⚠️ and between a corporation and its own shareholder that is more likely a
    **distribution** (§301/§1368 — basis reduction, then capital gain) **or COMPENSATION (W-2 wages,
    FICA)** than §61(a)(11) cancellation-of-debt income. **The character follows the capacity in
    which he received it, and the earlier "forgiven = income" wording dropped the FICA branch
    entirely**;
  - 🔴 **they were never bona fide loans at all** — and **this one has teeth, because
    recharacterising a 994,031 shareholder receivable as distributions across prior years would have
    consumed the very basis §2F-i is trying to measure.** ⛔ **The two findings interact; they are
    not separate problems.**
  **Nobody has asked, and it should be asked.**
- 🟠 **AND IT PUTS A FIFTH CHARACTER ON THE 2025 MONEY THAT §4A DOES NOT LIST.** This corporation
  has a demonstrated habit of running large shareholder loan balances. **The sum he received in 2025
  could be a LOAN DRAW, which is not income at all.** ⛔ **Not asserted — but it can no longer be
  excluded, and it is exactly the kind of thing the missing 2025 K-1 would settle.**

### 2D · ✅ The basis question — **SETTLED**; the missing Form 7203 is a separate matter and is NOT

> ✅ **SUPERSEDED IN PART, 2026-09-22 — READ §2F-i FIRST.** Lilian ruled the prior return is
> **accepted, not audited**, which settles the **basis figure**: opening 2025 stock basis is **ZERO**
> and the 537,926 is used as reported. ⛔ **Her ruling does NOT reach the MISSING FORM.** Whether a
> 2024 return filed by another firm should be amended because Form 7203 was required and not filed
> is **Julia's call**, and it is still open. **What follows is the reasoning that framed the
> question, kept because it is what makes the missing form a real issue.**

⛔ **No Form 7203 appears in the text extracted from the 44-page return** — the string `7203` occurs
**zero times** in 440,198 characters. ⚠️ **Bounded claim:** 4 of the 44 pages extracted only
partially (§6); none of the four is form-shaped, but the absence rests on the extraction.
⚠️ **And THREE were required, not one** — the basis limitation is per corporation and Form 7203 is
filed per S corporation. **All three K-1s carried losses.**

**It was required.** The Form 7203 instructions list four triggers, any one of which compels it, and
he meets the first: *"claiming a deduction for their share of an aggregate loss from an S
corporation"*. Schedule E page 2 states the same duty on its face — *"if you report a loss … you
must check the box in column (e) on line 28 and attach the required basis computation."*
⚠️ **The column (e) checkbox was not readable in THIS extraction.** *(Not a claim about PDFs in general — a flattened return often renders a tick as text.)*

**Why it matters: §1366(d)(1) caps the deductible loss at the shareholder's stock and debt basis.**
Deducting 630,737 in full, as nonpassive, asserts **630,737 of basis**. Nobody has verified it and
no form supports it.

🔑 **The two possible treatments were opposite in effect** — ✅ **and the LEFT column is the one this
return uses, by Lilian's ruling (§2F-i). The right column is kept only to show what accepting the
prior return avoided:**

| ✅ **If basis was sufficient (as filed) — THE ACCEPTED READING** | ⛔ *Not taken: if it was not* |
|---|---|
| A **net operating loss of 537,926** | A **§1366(d)(2) suspended loss** |
| Usable against **any** income | Released only when he has **BASIS** in that corporation again — from income, **a contribution or a shareholder loan** (§1366(d)(2)) |
| Capped at **80%** of taxable income *(computed without the NOL, QBI or §250 deductions)* | And only when basis is restored |
| Survives a disposal of the stock | ⛔ **GENERALLY dies with the shares** if basis is never restored — ⓘ *but see §1366(d)(3), which allows suspended losses in the post-termination transition period to the extent of stock basis, and a §1041 transfer to a spouse, which carries them to the transferee* |

⚠️ **The absence of the form is not proof the loss was wrong.** If he genuinely contributed that
capital or lent the company that money personally, the loss is allowable and what is missing is the
**form**, not the **basis**. ✅ **That is the reading the firm has adopted (§2F-i) — which leaves the
missing form standing on its own as a 2024 question for Julia.**

⛔ **The bank's debt does not count.** In an S corporation — unlike a partnership — entity-level
borrowing gives the shareholder no basis. **For a trucking company with financed equipment this is
the likeliest place for the figure to fail.**

### 2E · 🟠 §461(l) — COMPUTED 2026-09-21, and it NARROWS to one question

**Schedule 1 line 8p — the §461(l) excess-business-loss adjustment — is blank, and no Form 461
appears anywhere in the text extracted from the return.** Three independent searches, and then a
fourth proof that does not depend on the extraction at all:

1. The string `461` occurs **once** in 440,198 characters, and it is the printed label on line 8p.
2. The form's title, *"Limitation on Business Losses"*, occurs **zero** times.
3. Its **attachment sequence No. 64** does not appear.
4. 🔑 **AND THE ARITHMETIC FORBIDS ONE — this is the strong proof.** An excess-business-loss add-back
   lands on Schedule 1 line 8p, inside line 9 and therefore inside line 10. **Line 10 ties exactly to
   131 + 49,000 − 631,073 = (581,942) with nothing added back** (§5). **A Form 461 reporting an EBL
   would have broken that tie.**

⚠️ **Bound on proofs 1-3, and it is not cosmetic: 4 of the 44 pages extracted only partially (§6),
and THE SEQUENCE SCAN IS DEMONSTRABLY INCOMPLETE.** Schedule C carries attachment sequence **09**;
this return **contains** a Schedule C (Sch 1 line 3 = 131) whose header sits on one of those four
pages — **and 09 is not in the list the scan produced.** ⛔ **So a form sitting on a partial page can
be missed by exactly this method.** *(Proof 4 is unaffected — it rests on figures that were read.)*

⛔ **AN EARLIER VERSION LEFT THIS AS "FLAG FOR JULIA — THIS PAPER DOES NOT HOLD THE SPLIT." IT HOLDS
IT NOW. A SECOND VERSION THEN CALLED THE MATTER CLOSED. BOTH WERE WRONG: the computation is real,
and it resolves to ONE question rather than to an answer.**

**The 2024 rules, quoted from the 2024 Instructions for Form 461:**

> *"For 2024, the threshold amount is **$305,000 ($610,000 for taxpayers filing a joint return)**."*
> *"Such excess losses should be determined **without regard to any deductions, gross income, or
> gains attributable to any trade or business of performing services of an employee**."*

**Form 461 (2024), rebuilt from this return's figures:**

| Line | What it is | Value |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | Sch 1 line 3 — the spouse's Schedule C | **+131** 📖 |
| 3 | 1040 line 7 — capital gains | ⚠️ **never read** — see §6 |
| 4 | Sch 1 line 4 — Form 4797 | **+49,000** 📖 |
| 5 | Sch 1 line 5 — Schedule E | **(631,073)** 📖 |
| **9** | Combine lines 1-8 | **(581,942)** ✅ ties to Sch 1 line 10 |
| **10** | Income/gain on lines 1-8 **NOT** attributable to a trade or business | 🔴 **0 — THE LOAD-BEARING ASSUMPTION** |
| 14 | Line 9 + line 13 | **(581,942)** |
| 15 | *"Enter $305,000 (or $610,000 if married filing jointly)"* | **610,000** |
| **16** | Line 14 + line 15 — **negative means an EBL to report** | **+28,058 → nothing to report** ƒ |

✅ **The W-2 and the pension are out, and more cleanly than an earlier version said.** It is not that
an adjustment removes them: **Form 461 Part I never picks them up at all** — 1040 line 1z and line 5b
appear on none of lines 1-8 (lines 1 and 7 are *Reserved for future use*). ⓘ *And Form 172 line 7
reads 31,421, exactly the pension, which corroborates that interest, dividends and IRA lines are nil.*

## 🔴 THE ONE QUESTION THE ANSWER HANGS ON

**Line 10 removes any income on lines 1-8 that is NOT attributable to a trade or business.** The
whole result turns on the **49,000 Form 4797 gain** being a trade-or-business disposal **of his**.
⛔ **Form 4797 also reports gains on property that is not the taxpayer's trade-or-business asset —
the form's title is not the test, line 10 is.** And §7 of this very paper records that disposal as
**"not examined"**.

| If the 49,000 IS trade-or-business | If it is NOT |
|---|---|
| Line 10 = 0 · line 14 = (581,942) | Line 10 = 49,000 → line 13 = (49,000) · line 14 = **(630,942)** |
| **Line 16 = +28,058 → no EBL** | **Line 16 = (20,942) → an EBL of 20,942 on Sch 1 line 8p** |

⚖️ **AND HERE IS WHY THIS IS NOT ALARMING — the 2025 carryforward is 537,926 EITHER WAY.** A
disallowed excess business loss *"is treated as a net operating loss (NOL) carryover for subsequent
years."* So on the second branch: 2024 AGI rises by 20,942, the Form 172 NOL falls to **516,984**,
and the 20,942 arrives separately as an NOL carryover. **516,984 + 20,942 = 537,926.** ✅ **The
figure this paper exists to establish does not move. What moves is the presentation of the 2024
return.**

ⓘ **A robustness point that runs the same way:** the instructions' **Ordering Rules** apply at-risk,
then passive, then excess business loss. So if §2D's basis question resolves against the taxpayer,
the allowable loss is *smaller* — which makes the no-EBL conclusion **safer**, not shakier.

🟠 **AND SEPARATELY, THE FORM WAS OWED WHATEVER THE ARITHMETIC PRODUCED.** Under **Who Must File**:

> *"File Form 461 if you're a noncorporate taxpayer … and either (i) your net losses from all of
> your trades or businesses are more than $305,000 ($610,000 …), or **(ii) you would report a loss
> of more than $152,500 on any one of Form 461, lines 1 through 8**."*

**Line 5 carries (631,073).** ⛔ **So the form had to be filed even on the branch where it computes
to zero.** **Whether that is worth raising with the client, and whether a 2024 return filed by
another firm should be amended, is Julia's call — not this paper's.**

established:** §2B derived the 2024 wage figure by backing it out of the 1040 *(assuming five unread
lines are zero)*, and §2F-ii itself says **"corroborated, not confirmed"** — officer compensation on
Form 1125-E is a **deduction line**, not evidence a Form W-2 was issued. 🛑 **Nobody has seen a 2024
W-2.** 🔑 **This string goes to the IRS: it says what the corporation's own return says, and nothing
more.**
⚠️ **Only add it if Julia wants it** — the context is true and helpful, but volunteering is a choice.

has no money column** — the money grid sits below it with no line number of its own (§4A, and the
`tax-return-sop` skill singles this out). ⛔ **Naming line 28 as the place a figure is reported would
put the one trap this firm has written down into a string that goes to the IRS.**

🔑 **The first two lines are the instructions' sentence, verbatim and complete** — parentheses and all,
even though they name forms that do not apply here. **The rest is ours, and is what makes the form a
disclosure rather than a label.**

🟠 **There is a second canonical phrase and it is NOT ours.** If it were established that the
corporation **did not file**, the instructions require the sentence *"Partnership (S corporation,
Estate, Trust, or REMIC) return not filed."* — ⚠️ **that is the printed string; you instantiate it to
"S corporation return not filed."** ⛔ **We do not know that it failed to file.** ⓘ *If it is ever confirmed, that sentence is added — it does not replace the other.*

🟠 **The other two corporations — ⛔ THIS PARAGRAPH IS SUPERSEDED, 2026-09-23.** It reasoned that with

silent" — and disclosure is what preserves his position if a K-1 ever surfaces.**
⚠️ **The underlying risk this paragraph identified is unchanged and still live:** it rests entirely on
his being *"almost sure"* those two had no income, and **he has said he does not hold their returns.**
**A known risk, not a conclusion.**

### 3C · Form 8995 — the §199A carryforward that is typed by hand

📎 **Read off `f8995.pdf` and `i8995.pdf` on 2026-09-22 — the 2025 edition.** ⛔ **An earlier draft of
this subsection had no provenance line and carried a quotation that does not exist in the
instructions; both are corrected below.**

✅ **Form 8995, not 8995-A.** The simplified form is used where taxable income before the QBI deduction
is at or below **$394,600 (MFJ, 2025)**, printed on the form's own face. **Comfortably met** once the
NOL runs against the year's income.

> 🟠 **LINE 1 IS SUPERSEDED BY §4E — 2026-09-23. The rest of this table stands.** It names **Midwest
> Expedited Corp, EIN 47-5147826, QBI 200,053**, which was right when the money was an S-corporation
> distributive share. ⛔ **Under Julia's route the qualified trade or business is HIS SOLE
> PROPRIETORSHIP.** Line 1 becomes:
>
> | Column | Under the Schedule C route |
> |---|---|
> | **(a)** name | 🔴 **The Schedule C activity, not the corporation** — ⛔ **do not write "Midwest Expedited Corp"**, he does not own its QBI. **Follows Schedule C item A**, which is itself not established (§4F-i) |
> | **(b)** TIN | 🔴 **HIS SSN — ⛔ NOT blank.** 📎 *"Enter on line 1(b) the employer identification number (EIN). **If you don't have an EIN, enter your social security number (SSN)** or individual taxpayer identification number (ITIN)."* ⚠️ **Schedule C item D stays blank; Form 8995 1(b) does not** — different instructions. 🔑 **Typed at the keyboard from the client record; ⛔ the number is NOT written into this paper.** ⓘ *A blank 1(b) is authorised only for an aggregation group, which this is not* |
> | **(c)** QBI | 🔴 **NOT Schedule C line 31, and NOT 200,053** |
>
> 🛑 **THE (c) FIGURE IS THE TRAP, and it is a reduction the software may or may not make for you.**
> 📎 **2025 Instructions for Form 8995, *Determining Your Qualified Business Income*, verbatim:**
>
> > *"To figure the total amount of QBI, you must consider all items that are attributable to the
> > trade or business. This includes, but isn't limited to, unreimbursed partnership expenses,
> > business interest expense, **deductible part of self-employment tax, self-employment health
> > insurance deduction, and contributions to qualified retirement plans.**"*
>
> 📎 **And, in terms:** *"The net gain or loss reported on your Schedule C (Form 1040) **isn't
> automatically included in your QBI.**"*
>
> 🧮 **So (c) = Schedule C line 31 − Schedule 1 line 15 (half the SE tax) − Schedule 1 line 17 (the
> §162(l) health deduction).**
> 🔴 **THE SUBTRACTION IS NOW ONE TERM, NOT TWO — corrected 2026-09-23.** ✅ **Line 17 is ZERO** (§4G:
> no health insurance), **so only half the SE tax comes off.**
> ⛔ **AND THE OLD FIGURE IS DEAD TWICE OVER.** An earlier version said *"overstated by 24,230
> (9,363 + 14,867)"*. **The 14,867 is gone, and the 9,363 is stale too** — §4G adds **10,944** (line 9)
> and **2,389** (line 16b) to the Schedule C, which lowers line 31, the SE tax and therefore half of it.
> 📌 **Do not compare ATX's column (c) against any number written here.** 🔑 **Read Schedule 1 line 15
> off the finished return and subtract THAT from line 31.** 🛠️ **Then tell me what ATX put in (c)** —
> if it carries line 31 gross, the QBI is overstated by exactly that line-15 figure and nothing flags it.
> ✅ **Line 3 — the prior-year qualified business loss carryforward — is UNAFFECTED and still the line
> nobody looks at.**

missing, and the result looks entirely reasonable without it. 🔑 **The tell: if Form 1040 line 13a shows
a sizeable deduction, go back to line 3.**

the 2024 instructions.** The real sentence is **line 16's**, and it is quoted correctly above. ✅ **The
substance was right** — line 3 is the right line and the 2024 loss does carry as negative QBI — **only
the wording and its address were invented.**

### ⚖️ Decision 5 — the vehicle, and what closing it actually closes

_(Lilian, 2026-09-22: **"en cuanto al vehículo, vamos a olvidar eso por ahora. Julia me dijo que no es
algo que vamos a reportar en esta declaración."**)_

**What the client said**, 2026-08-17, in his own words: *"I sold one of my vehicles in 2025 but it
just close credit (balance on credit) I didn't make any profit etc"* — **he used the proceeds to clear
the loan and says there was no gain.**

✅ **Closed as an open item.** The question comes off the client list and off §8; nobody chases the
sale document.
⚠️ **What closing it does NOT establish, recorded so nobody reads the closure as a finding:** *"no
profit"* is **the client's characterisation, not a computation** — paying off a loan says nothing about
gain, which is proceeds against **adjusted basis**. **If the vehicle had ever been depreciated, a gain
could exist on a sale that cleared no cash.** ⛔ **That test has not been run and is not being run** —
this is a decision about what goes on the return, not a conclusion about the tax.
🔵 **And it narrows by itself if §2F-i's reading holds:** the 2024 Form 4797 gain traced to the
**corporation's** §179 disposition, not to a vehicle of his — so the vehicles in this story may be the
company's throughout, in which case a sale is the corporation's event and never his.

### ⚖️ Decision 6 — the Marketplace assumption is now a PROCEDURE, not a question

**It does not change decision 2; it closes the follow-up.** Nobody chases the 1095-A, nobody asks the
client again, and the broker question comes off the list. **The return is transmitted; a rejection
under F8962-070 is what turns the form into a requirement.**
⚠️ **All THREE of §4's limits still bind, and the third is the one that matters most now that the
reject has been promoted to "the only verification this return has":**
1. ⛔ **E-FILE ONLY.** A paper-filed return is accepted and followed up by mail — **the test simply does
   not exist there.** 🔴 **So this ruling makes e-filing a REQUIREMENT of the return, not a preference.**
   **If it has to go on paper, say so and the assumption goes back to being untested.**
2. ⛔ **Bypassable** with an `ACA Explanation` PDF the software will offer. **Do not use it.**
3. ⚠️ **A reject says LESS than "we were wrong about him."** It says the IRS's e-file database shows
   APTC paid for someone on this return — **a claim about the database, not about his memory.** ⛔ **And
   the converse is weaker still: no reject does NOT prove there was no Marketplace plan**, only that no
   advance credit was paid. **An unsubsidised exchange plan still produces a 1095-A and rejects nothing.**

### ⚖️ Decision 2 — the assumption, its basis, and why it is safer than it sounds

**The basis is the client's own final answer**, and the sequence matters: on several occasions he
said he **did not know** whether his cover was Marketplace; Lilian then asked him to check with a
broker (*he does not have the broker's number*) and to call the Marketplace himself (*he did not*);
and he then stated that he had **no Marketplace coverage in 2025**. ⚠️ **Lilian's own reading, and
it is recorded because it is the honest reason: he shows no appetite for making the enquiry.**

⚠️ **It contradicts his own 2025 organizer, which answers Marketplace — that contradiction stands
on the record and is not resolved by this decision.**

🟢 **THERE IS A PARTIAL SELF-TEST AT FILING — but an earlier version of this paragraph overstated
it three ways, and the corrections matter.** If he *did* have Marketplace cover **with an advance
premium tax credit**, the IRS holds the 1095-A and the return is rejected under business rule
**F8962-070**. ⚠️ **Now the three limits:**
- ⛔ **IT IS E-FILE ONLY.** The IRS states: *"Because business rule F8962-070 only applies to
  **electronically filed** returns, these **paper-filed** returns will be accepted and the IRS will
  generally follow up by mail."* **This return is already past 2026-04-15 and may well be paper-filed
  — in which case the test does not exist.**
- 🔴 **IT IS BYPASSABLE, AND THE BYPASS WILL BE OFFERED.** The IRS tells taxpayers who believe the
  form is not required to attach *"a PDF attachment titled **'ACA Explanation'**"* and the return
  goes through. **A preparer who meets F8962-070 in the software will be offered exactly that.**
  ⛔ **DO NOT USE IT HERE.** A reject on this return is the signal we are relying on; suppressing it
  destroys the only check there is.
- ⚠️ **And a reject says less than "we were wrong about him."** It says the **IRS's e-file database**
  shows APTC paid for someone on this return — which is the fact that matters, but it is a claim
  about the database, not about his memory.
⛔ **The case it does NOT catch either way:** an exchange plan bought with **no** advance credit.
That produces a 1095-A and no reject; the cost is a **forfeited premium tax credit** — his money left
behind rather than a penalty. ⚠️ **One forward cost even so: failure to reconcile APTC can lead the
Marketplace to deny an advance credit in a LATER year.** ⓘ *Form 8962 is required where APTC was
paid, or where the taxpayer claims the credit.*

### ⛔ ~~Decision 3~~ — **REVERSED BY DECISION 7, 2026-09-23. Everything below is the old position.**

> 🛑 **This subsection asserts, with a ✅ on it, that the business is the CORPORATION's and that a
> Schedule C is ruled out.** ⛔ **Julia reversed exactly that on 2026-09-23: the money and the
> client's own expenses go on a Schedule C on HIS return** (§4E). ⚠️ **The decisions TABLE row 3 is
> struck; this prose sits 77 lines below it under its own heading, which is where anyone navigating
> to "Decision 3" arrives — so it is marked here too.**
> ✅ **What survives, and it is the sharpest thing in it:** the cost it names — **did the corporation
> actually deduct those expenses?** ⛔ **If he paid them personally and was never reimbursed, nobody
> deducted them**, which under the NEW route is the argument FOR putting them on his return, not
> against. 🔑 **Same fact, opposite conclusion** (§8 item 0b).

✅ **What it settled: the business is the CORPORATION'S, not his.** That is the premise of *"the
expenses are deductible on the 1120-S"*, and it is the most useful thing in the instruction, because
it **rules out two reporting routes outright** (§4A below).

⚠️ **What it costs, and it should be a chosen cost rather than a discovered one:** it assumes the
corporation **did** deduct those costs on its own return. **If it did not — because he paid them
personally out of the gross and was never reimbursed — then nobody deducts them at all.** ⛔ **We cannot check this for 2025 — we have the
2024 1120-S (§2F) but not the 2025 one and not the books.** ⓘ *The 2024 cost structure is now
visible and gives a partial test.*

⚠️ **And it does not decide the CHARACTER of the money**, which is what §4A is for.

## 4A · 🔑 HOW TO REPORT THE MONEY FROM MIDWEST EXPEDITED

_(Lilian's question, 2026-09-21: with no K-1, how does the money get onto the return at all?
**Rewritten the same day after review** — the first version framed two routes as alternatives, and
that is not how an S corporation works.)_

### ⛔ First, the route that must NOT be taken — 🔴 **AND IT IS THE ROUTE JULIA CHOSE ON 2026-09-23**

> 🛑 **READ THIS TABLE AS THE PREPARER'S OBJECTION, NOT AS THE PLAN.** §4E records Julia's reversal.
> ⛔ **The objection is not withdrawn and it is not softened** — [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/)
> **§4C**: where a working paper records an objection that was overruled, **the objection travels to
> the signer WITH the decision.** 📌 **This is that objection, in the words it was first written in.**

| Route | Why it dies |
|---|---|
| **Schedule C** | It would declare the business **HIS**, the opposite of decision 3's own premise. ⛔ **And it would add self-employment tax** — ⚠️ **NOT a flat 15.3%, which an earlier version said:** SE tax is **12.4% on up to the year's wage base ($176,100 for 2025) plus 2.9% with no ceiling**, charged on **92.35%** of net earnings, and half of it is deductible under §164(f). On a sum this size that is still **tens of thousands of dollars** of tax an S-corp shareholder does not owe. **It is the expensive mistake available here.** |

🔵 **A SECOND GROUND WAS ADDED ON 2026-09-23 AND IS THE STRONGER OF THE TWO** *(§4E)*: the 2025
Instructions for Schedule SE exclude income received **"for performing services as an employee"** from
net earnings from self-employment. 🔑 **The first ground says the Schedule C is expensive. The second
says it is wrong.** ⛔ **Neither has been answered.**

### 🔑 THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS: THIS IS NOT AN EITHER/OR

**A real Schedule K-1 would report BOTH**, and so must this return:

| | What it is | Where it goes |
|---|---|---|
| **Box 1** | His **allocated share** of the corporation's ordinary business income | **Schedule E page 2, Part II, column (k)** — nonpassive |
| **Box 16, code D** | **The cash the corporation actually distributed to him** | **Not an income line at all** — it adjusts basis, and only shows on the return if it exceeds basis |

⛔ **The first version of this section offered these as a choice. They are two different things that
both happened**: whatever his allocated share turns out to be, **the cash that moved is a
distribution.** Reporting only the income silently drops the distribution; reporting only the
distribution drops the income.

### 🟢 And the ORDER defuses the capital-gain fear the first version raised

**§1367, quoted from the Shareholder's Instructions for Schedule K-1 (1120-S):** basis is increased
by *"all income (including tax-exempt income) reported on Schedule K-1"*, and **then** decreased by
*"property distributions (including cash)"*. **Income first, distributions after.**

So on this fact pattern, even starting from **zero** basis:

| Step | Basis |
|---|---|
| Opening (assume 2024's loss consumed it) | **0** |
| **+ box 1 income reported** | **+ the amount reported** |
| **− box 16D distribution** | **back toward 0** |
| **Capital gain under §1368(b)(2)** | ⛔ **NONE, to the extent the income covers the distribution** |

⛔ **An earlier version said "if basis is zero the whole sum is capital gain." That is WRONG and is
struck** — it ignored the ordering, and it was then used to argue the distribution route was
unusable. **It is not unusable; it is half of the answer.**

### 🔑 AND IT REACHES THE FILE'S BIGGEST OPEN QUESTION

**Reporting box 1 income INCREASES his stock basis by that amount (§1367(a)(1)).** Two consequences
nobody had drawn:

1. ✅ **It is what makes the distribution non-taxable** (above).
2. ✅ **It is what keeps the opening basis of ZERO harmless** (§2F-i). ⛔ **It no longer frees
   suspended losses, because there are none** — Lilian's ruling accepts that the 2024 return
   deducted the loss in full and suspended nothing. ⚠️ **But the mechanism still matters on the one
   branch §2F-i leaves open: if he ever had DEBT basis, the year's net increase restores the DEBT
   first (Reg. §1.1367-2(c)) and stock basis stays at zero — which would make the distribution
   taxable after all.**

### 🟠 THE ROUTE THE FIRST VERSION DISMISSED ON THE WRONG GROUND — and Julia must be told she is choosing

**Reasonable compensation.** The IRS's published position on S-corp shareholders: *"To the extent
gross receipts are generated by the shareholder's personal services, then payments to the
shareholder-employee should be classified as **wages** that are subject to employment taxes,"* and
*"the intent to limit wages is not a controlling factor."*

⚠️ **He worked in this company in 2025** (client file §5). **So reporting the whole sum as box 1
income with ZERO FICA is a defensible position — but it is a POSITION, and the signer is entitled to
know she is taking it.**

⛔ **And the mechanical objection the first version raised was aimed at the wrong form.** It said a
wage treatment *"requires withholding figures he does not have"* — true of **Form 4852** (substitute
W-2). **It is not true of Form 8919**, *"used to figure and report your share of the uncollected
social security and Medicare taxes due on your compensation if you were an employee but were treated
as an independent contractor by your employer"* — which needs **no withholding figures at all**, and
whose reason codes include one for having filed **Form SS-8** without a reply. ⚠️ **Read the reason
codes off the current form; the fit is imperfect** — he is a shareholder-employee whose payments were
never reported at all, not a misclassified contractor. **But the route EXISTS and had to be on the
record rather than declared impossible.**

### ⛔ ~~📋 What to actually do~~ — **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23. DO NOT BUILD THE RETURN FROM THIS LIST.**

> 🛑 **This is the only imperative build-the-return list in §4A, and every step of it describes the
> route Julia reversed.** ⛔ **There is no box-1 entry, no box-16D entry, no Form 7203 and no Schedule
> E on this return** (§4E). ✅ **Step 4 — put the wage question to Julia before filing — is the one
> thing here that SURVIVES, and it hardened: it now gates transmission** (§9A ④).
> 🔑 **The live build list is §4F.** ⓘ *Kept because it is what gets done if she reverses back.*

1. **Report box 1 ordinary business income** on Schedule E Part II, nonpassive, at the figure Julia
   directs — see the health warning below.
2. **Record the distribution** (box 16D) at the cash actually received, and **run the basis
   computation on Form 7203** — which is required anyway once a non-dividend distribution is
   reported.
3. **Attach Form 8082**, explanation *"Schedule K-1 … not received"* (§4).
4. **Put the wage question to Julia** before filing, as a position she is choosing.

🛠️ **Entry route:** none of this is typed on Schedule E. In the software you create a **Schedule K-1
(1120-S) input** for Midwest Expedited Corp, put the figure in **ordinary business income (box 1)**,
mark the activity **nonpassive**, and enter the **distribution** in the box-16D field; Schedule E and
Form 7203 are outputs. ⚠️ *General shape, not verified in ATX — confirm on screen.*

### 🟠 A FIFTH POSSIBLE CHARACTER, opened 2026-09-21 by the corporation's own balance sheet

**This company lends its shareholders money — a lot of it.** Schedule L shows *loans to
shareholders* of **994,031** falling to **34,213** during 2024 (§2F-iii). ⛔ **So the sum he received
in 2025 could be a LOAN DRAW, which is not income at all** — and a loan *to* him creates no basis,
it is the opposite of the one that would. **Not asserted. But it can no longer be excluded, and it
is another thing the missing 2025 K-1 would settle at a stroke.**

### 🔴 The health warning on the figure itself, unchanged and still the weakest point

**The sum he received is CASH. Box 1 is an ALLOCATED SHARE.** They are not the same number, and if
the corporation deducted its expenses — decision 3's own premise — **his share is LOWER than the
cash.** ⚠️ **So putting the cash figure in box 1 probably OVERSTATES his income.**
⛔ **And "it errs toward overpaying" is NOT a safe harbour** — an overstated return is a wrong
return, disclosed on Form 8082 as an estimate. **Julia should be told the overstatement is something
she is choosing, not a conservatism that costs nothing.**
🔑 **When the K-1 arrives the amendment is near-certain rather than contingent.**

### ⚠️ Three consequences of Variant A that have no home elsewhere

- **§199A.** Box 1 income is QBI (trucking is not an SSTB), so the software will offer a deduction —
  ⛔ **but 2024's qualified business loss carries forward as NEGATIVE QBI and offsets it**, on **Form
  8995 line 3**: *"Include here the qualified portion of trade or business (loss) carryforward allowed
  in calculating taxable income in the current year, **even if the loss was from a trade or business
  that is no longer in existence**."* **The 2025 deduction is very likely nil — but only if the
  carryforward is ENTERED.** ⚠️ **An earlier version of this bullet misquoted that instruction — §3C
  has the verified wording and the whole form line by line.** *(Added to §7.)*
- **§6654 estimated tax.** The NOL shelters only **80%** of taxable income, so real tax arises on
  income that had **no withholding** — an underpayment-penalty exposure nobody has costed.
- **⛔ ~~§8 item 7 is now conditional~~ — STRUCK 2026-09-23.** It said *"the NOL does not reduce
  self-employment tax"* was **moot under decision 3, since there is no SE tax**. 🔴 **Decision 7 put a
  Schedule C on the return, so there IS SE tax — ⚠️ **roughly 17,000–27,000 of it** *(≈ 16,841 –
  27,194, §4E's banner — an earlier "19,000–27,000" here was stale at the bottom after the vehicle
  deduction and the zeroed health insurance landed on 2026-09-23)* — **and it is now the largest
  number on the return.** §8 item 7 reads the opposite way and is the current one.

⛔ **And Illinois does not go away** — ⚠️ **but this paragraph was written for the §4B route and is
now WRONG AS WRITTEN.** It says *"we report S-corp income whose Illinois-source portion is unknown"*;
⛔ **no S-corp income is reported under Julia's route at all.**
🔴 **AND NOTHING IN §4E OR §4F SAYS WHAT A SCHEDULE C DOES TO ILLINOIS — it is a DIFFERENT analysis
with a possibly different answer.** A K-1 share sources by the corporation's apportionment; **a sole
proprietorship sources by where the business was carried on**, which is a fact about him, not about
the company. ✅ **What survives unchanged: an amended federal return does not file a state return
that was never filed** (§4).
⚠️ **AND IT IS NOT ALL-OR-NOTHING** — a nonresident carrying on business in more than one state
**apportions his OWN business income**, so *"where the business was carried on"* decides whether
Illinois has a claim and on what factors, **not a yes/no.** ⛔ **Do not read it as "he wasn't in
Illinois, so nothing."**
🔴 **A TRUCKING-SPECIFIC POINT RUNS THE OTHER WAY AND IS UNVERIFIED:** *49 U.S.C. §14503* is
generally understood to bar any state but the residence state from taxing an **interstate
motor-carrier EMPLOYEE's** compensation — 🔑 **and it reaches employees, not self-employed
owner-operators.** ⚠️ **If so, the Schedule C may CREATE the multi-state exposure that a wage
treatment would have closed.** ⛔ **Nobody has read the statute; not asserted, and not to be relied
on either way** — 📌 **it is on the check-before-Julia-prices-it list (§4E).**
📌 **Open, and nobody has put any of this to Julia.**

## 4B · ⛔ ~~THE ONE DECISION INSIDE A SINGLE LINE~~ — **MOOT 2026-09-23. NOT A QUESTION FOR JULIA.**

> 🛑 **SUPERSEDED — and do NOT take this back to Julia as an open decision.** It asks her to choose
> between two ways of reporting **Form 7203 line 6**, a line on a form that **is not filed under her
> Schedule C route** (§3A, §4E). ⛔ **Variant A and variant B are both dead**, and §7 records that the
> 2026 opening basis is zero for a different reason entirely: **the 2025 return says nothing about
> basis at all.**
> ✅ **What survives and is worth reading:** the §1368(b)(2) / §1367 ordering analysis, and
> ⚠️ **"THREE THINGS THIS DECISION DOES NOT ESCAPE"** below — **the first of which, that the money's
> true character is unknown, is what §4E's objection is built on.**
> 🔑 **Live again the moment Julia reverses back.**

**Raised 2026-09-22, while writing §3A's table. It is Julia's, it costs nothing in 2025 tax, and it
sets a figure a preparer will type next year without reading any of this.**

**The only established fact is that he received 200,053 in cash.** Whether that is an *allocated
share* (box 1) or a *distribution* (box 16D) is unknown, because there is no K-1 — ⛔ **and a real K-1
would report both** (§4A).

| | Line 3a | Line 6 | **Line 15 — what carries** | 2025 tax |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ **A — recommended** *(income AND distribution)* | 200,053 | **200,053** | **0** | Income raises basis **before** the distribution reduces it (§1367's order) → **no §1368(b)(2) gain** |
| **B** *(income only)* | 200,053 | 0 | **200,053** | **Identical** |

🔑 **The 2025 tax is the same either way — tested, not assumed.** Checked against §199A *(distributions
do not touch QBI)*, the NOL's 80% limit *(a distribution within basis is not taxable income)*, the NIIT
*(nonpassive S-corp income is not net investment income, and neither is a nondividend distribution
within basis)*, SE tax and §6654. **The whole difference lands in 2026.**

✅ **Why A:** the cash **left the corporation and reached him** — that is the one documented event.
⛔ **B states that he earned 200,053 and took nothing out**, which contradicts the only fact we hold.
🔑 **And A is the CLIENT-ADVERSE choice** — it hands 2026 an opening basis of zero instead of 200,053.
**Worth saying to Julia in those words: we are not recommending it because it helps him.**

⚠️ **One correction to an earlier draft of this section:** it said B *"hands next year an opening basis
of 200,053 that nothing supports."* ⛔ **Overstated** — under B that basis is supported by exactly the
box-1 income being reported. **What is unsupported under B is the assumption that no distribution
occurred.**

⚠️ **The honest objection to A:** it invents an *allocation* equal to the cash, so the return asserts
two figures where we know one. **That is already true of box 1 under decision 3** — A does not add a
second estimate, it keeps the two halves of one estimate consistent.

### ⛔ THREE THINGS THIS DECISION DOES NOT ESCAPE

1. 🔴 **The no-gain conclusion is CONDITIONAL, and §8 item 2 holds both conditions.** It survives only
   while **2025 is an income year** *(a chosen reporting figure, not a fact)* and only while **debt
   basis really is zero** *(a reading of a balance sheet at two dates — §7 marks it 🟠, and if he did
   lend, the year's increase restores the DEBT first and the distribution becomes taxable)*.
   ⛔ **The table above states "no §1368(b)(2) gain" flatly. Read it with these.**
2. 🟠 **§4A's fifth character is not in the table at all.** The cash **could be a LOAN DRAW, which is
   not income and not a distribution** (§8 0B). A two-row table excludes it by construction. **It is
   excluded from the table, not from the facts.**
3. 🛑 **UNDER VARIANT B, FORM 7203 IS NOT FILED AT ALL.** Its four triggers are: claiming a loss
   deduction, **receiving a non-dividend distribution**, disposing of stock, or receiving a loan
   repayment *(`i7203.pdf`, Who Must File)*. ⛔ **Under B none applies** — so §3A's entire table is
   conditional on A, and **Julia's choice removes a form from the return.** ⓘ *The instructions add
   that it is worth completing and retaining even when not required, precisely to keep the basis
   consistent year to year.*

⛔ **Julia's, not this paper's** — because line 15 becomes line 1 of the 2026 Form 7203, and by then
the reasoning will be in a deleted session.
