## 4C · 🔴 ZERO WAGES AND A LARGE DISTRIBUTION — the reasonable-compensation exposure, and how it is actually detected

_(Lilian's question, 2026-09-22: **"¿pudiera esto saltar alguna red flag? ¿Hay alguna forma de que el
auditor detecte esto: que el dueño de S-Corp no tuvo salario y, sin embargo, sí tuvo distribuciones?
¿Crees que esto nos pueda llevar a una auditoría?"** She then proposed the right next step herself.)_

### ⚖️ First, what this return actually asserts

> 🛑 **THIS WHOLE SECTION DESCRIBES THE ROUTE JULIA REVERSED ON 2026-09-23** — read it for the
> DETECTION analysis and the payroll question, which both survive and which §9A ③ carries forward.
> ⛔ **The return no longer reports a distribution at all**, so every reference below to *"box 1"*,
> *"box 16D"*, *"Form 7203 line 6"* and *"detection route 2"* is about a form this return does not
> file. 🔑 **The exposure did NOT disappear with the presentation** — §4E says why, in terms.

⛔ **It is NOT "all the money as distributions."** Under decision 3 the return reports **box 1 ordinary
business income** of 200,053 **and**, under §4B variant A, **a distribution of the same amount**. The
wage line is **zero** because he holds no W-2.
🔑 **The question is still exactly right**, because what an examiner sees is: *a shareholder-employee of
an S corporation, services performed, **no wages**, and a distribution.* **That is the classic profile.**

### 🔍 HOW IT IS DETECTED — four routes, and three of them do not need an audit

| # | Where the pattern is visible | Whose return | Needs a human? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Form 1120-S page 1 line 7 *Compensation of officers* + Form 1125-E** *(which names each officer with **% of stock, % of time** and the amount paid)* against **Schedule K line 16d distributions** and each **K-1 box 16D** | ⛔ **the CORPORATION's — we do not prepare it** | **No.** Both figures sit on one return; the comparison is mechanical |
| 2 | 🔴 **HIS Form 7203 line 6** — a distribution of 200,053 against **line 1 opening basis of zero** — beside **Form 1040 line 1a wages of ZERO** | **ours** | **No** |
| 3 | **K-1 ↔ 1040 data matching.** If the corporation files an 1120-S, the K-1 it files is matched to his return | both | **No** |
| 4 | **Form 8082 itself.** We are affirmatively telling the IRS the entity did not furnish a K-1 | **ours** | **No** — ⚠️ **this return is already more visible than an ordinary one** |
| 🔴 **5** | **W-2 / information-return matching.** ⛔ **THE ONE THAT WAS MISSING, AND IT IS THE MOST MECHANICAL OF ALL.** If a W-2 **was** filed with the SSA and never furnished to him, **Form 1040 line 1a of ZERO is matched against it automatically** and produces a CP2000 | **ours** | **No — and it is not discretionary** |

🛑 **ROUTE 5 IS A DIFFERENT KIND OF PROBLEM FROM THE OTHER FOUR.** Routes 1–4 make the return
**visible**. ⛔ **Route 5 makes it WRONG** — an omitted W-2 is an understatement, not a presentation.
🔑 **Which is why §8's payroll item cannot be a question "carried into the return": it has to be
answered BEFORE filing.** ⚠️ **And it cuts against the drafted Form 8082 paragraph below**, which
would otherwise sit on the return beside an omitted W-2.
ⓘ ⚠️ *Routes 1 and 3 need the corporation to have FILED, which §3B itself treats as unknown. And
routes 1 and 2 are stated as mechanism but are **inference about IRS systems, not observation** —
route 5 and route 3's K-1 matching are the documented ones. Form 1125-E is also only required where
total receipts reach $500,000, which is comfortably met here.*

🔑 **THE ONE THAT MATTERS FOR US IS ROUTE 2, AND IT IS A CONSEQUENCE OF §4B.** Under **variant A** we
attach a Form 7203 whose line 6 states the distribution. Under **variant B** no Form 7203 is filed at
all *(§4B item 3)* and **the distribution appears nowhere on his return.**
⛔ **THAT IS NOT A REASON TO CHOOSE B, and this paper does not offer it as one.** Choosing a
presentation because it shows less is not a filing position — the reason to pick A or B is which one
is **true**. 📌 **It is recorded because Julia is choosing between them and is entitled to know that
one of them is more visible than the other.**

### ⚖️ DOES IT LEAD TO AN AUDIT? — what can honestly be said, and what cannot

✅ **What is solid:** reasonable compensation for S-corporation shareholder-employees is a
long-standing, publicly stated IRS compliance priority, and the IRS's position is the one §4A already
quotes — *"payments to the shareholder-employee should be classified as **wages**"*, and *"the intent
to limit wages is not a controlling factor."* **The profile on this return is the textbook one.**
⛔ **What cannot be said:** whether this return will be selected. **Selection criteria are not public,
and a session that gives a probability is inventing one.**

🔑 **MOST OF THE EXPOSURE IS THE CORPORATION'S — but ⛔ TWO ROUTES REACH HIM PERSONALLY, and an
earlier draft of this section named neither.**

**What is the corporation's:** the employer share, the unfiled 941s and 940, the missing W-2s and
their penalties. **It is the employer, and this firm does not prepare its return.**

| 🔴 Route that reaches HIM | Why it is live here | Why it is not a reason to change the 1040 |
|---|---|---|
| **§6672 — the Trust Fund Recovery Penalty** | A **responsible person** who wilfully fails to collect and pay over withheld income tax and the **employee share** of FICA is personally liable for **100% of the trust-fund portion**. ⚠️ **The responsible-person facts are already in this file:** 50% shareholder, **officer**, recorded on Form 1125-E as devoting **100% of his time** to the business (§2F-ii). ⛔ **And it does not wait for the corporation to be insolvent.** 🔑 **Being locked out is a real defence for the months he was locked out — it is no answer at all for the months he ran the company** | It is an **employment-tax** exposure of the corporation's officers. **Nothing on his Form 1040 creates or cures it**, and no filing choice here changes it. ⛔ **But he is entitled to know it exists** |
| **§3101 — his own half of FICA** | ⚠️ **An earlier draft had this backwards.** It said his half "can reach him if the corporation cannot pay." **It is his regardless.** Under **§3509** the corporation's assessed liability for the employee share is *reduced* (20%, or 40% where no information return was filed), and **§3509(d) expressly does NOT relieve the employee of his own §3101 liability**, nor may the employer recover it from him. ⓘ *And §3509 does not apply at all where the failure is due to **intentional disregard** (§3509(c)) — which is the live argument in a zero-salary S-corp case* | 🔑 **This is precisely why Form 8919 exists** — see below, where it turns out to be **available**, not unavailable |

- **His income tax barely moves.** Wages and box 1 S-corp income are both ordinary income to him.
- ⚠️ **§199A** would shrink *(wages are not QBI)* — **likely moot here**, since the 2024 carryforward
  probably drives the deduction to nil anyway.

🟠 **SO THE HONEST SUMMARY IS NARROWER THAN "VISIBILITY, NOT LIABILITY", AND THAT PHRASE IS STRUCK.**
✅ **True:** nothing the firm puts on this 1040 creates the corporation's employment-tax liability, and
no presentation of it cures the §6672 exposure either.
⛔ **False, and it was written:** that there is therefore no personal exposure. **There is, it is
large, and it is his.**
⚠️ **Also struck: "there is no version of this return that hides the pattern honestly."** ⛔ **This
paper's own §4B variant B does exactly that** — no Form 7203, and the distribution appears nowhere.
**B is still on the table.** 🔑 **The defensible sentence is the narrow one: B is not to be chosen
BECAUSE it shows less — but it is dishonest to claim the option does not exist while offering it.**

### 🛑 AND THE PART THIS SECTION FIRST LEFT OUT: THE FIRM'S OWN OBLIGATIONS

⛔ **An earlier draft ended the analysis at "we are creating visibility, not liability." That was the
firm reassuring itself, and it is struck.** Three things are owed here, and they are **preparer-level,
not client-level** — they do not depend on what Julia decides to write on the form.

1. 🔴 **CIRCULAR 230 §10.21 — ADVISING THE CLIENT IS NOT OPTIONAL.** A practitioner who knows a client
   has not complied with the revenue laws **must promptly advise the client of the noncompliance and
   of its consequences.** **The firm knows:** services performed, cash taken, no wages reported,
   employment returns and W-2s almost certainly unfiled, and **a §6672 route pointing at him
   personally.** ⛔ **The one sentence about it is currently framed as "a disclosure choice" that is
   "Julia's to approve." That is the right framing for the FORM 8082 WORDING and the wrong framing
   for TELLING HIM.** 🔑 **He gets told either way.**
2. 🟠 **CIRCULAR 230 §10.34(c) — the penalties conversation, on the record.** The practitioner must
   inform the client of the penalties reasonably likely to apply, and of the opportunity to avoid
   them by disclosure. ⚠️ **This return has TWO material figures that are chosen rather than
   measured**: box 1 *(§4A concedes it is a proxy that probably overstates)* and the distribution
   *(§4B variant A sets it equal to the same cash)* — and §4A keeps a **fifth character** open in
   which the money is a loan draw and neither figure is right. 🔑 **Form 8082 plus the word
   "estimate" is not the same as having had that conversation.**
3. ⚖️ **DECLINING TO SIGN BELONGS IN JULIA'S OPTION SET — if only to be rejected.** ⛔ **It has never
   been written down as an option, and a set of choices that omits the most conservative one is not a
   set of choices.**
   🔑 **The honest sentence, and it should be Julia's to accept or refuse in these words:** *"We are
   signing a return two of whose material figures we invented, because the client cannot obtain the
   real ones, and we are disclosing that on Form 8082."* ✅ **That is defensible** — it is what Form
   8082 exists for, and the alternative is that he does not file at all. ⛔ **But it should be written
   that way, so the signer is choosing it rather than inheriting it.**

### 🟢 THE MITIGATION IS ALREADY TRUE AND IS CURRENTLY UNSTATED

**The reason he has no wages in 2025 is not a compensation strategy — he was locked out.** He worked
in the company in the first months of the year and was then denied all access (§0, client file §5).
📌 **RECOMMENDED, and it is Julia's call because it is a disclosure choice:** add one sentence to the
Form 8082 Part III explanation (§3B) saying he was a shareholder-employee who **received W-2 wages
from this same corporation in the prior year** and was denied access during 2025.
✅ **Why it helps:** it answers, on the face of the return, the exact question the pattern provokes —
and **the prior year corroborates it**: Form 1125-E named him at 41,795 of officer compensation for
2024 (§2F-ii). ⚠️ **A company that ran payroll for him last year is not a company that has never paid
him.**

### 🔑 LILIAN'S PROPOSAL IS THE RIGHT NEXT STEP — and it can be settled WITHOUT relying on his memory

_(Her words: **"tal vez deberíamos preguntarle al cliente si de alguna manera alguna parte de este
income fue corrido como un payroll… tal vez si se corrió payroll, este dinero le entró como un
depósito directo."**)_ ⓘ *Read as: he did NOT give us a W-2 for the year — the rest of the sentence
and the whole file only make sense that way; flagged rather than assumed.*

🔵 **AND THE FILE ALREADY ARGUES IN HER FAVOUR.** The corporation **ran payroll for him in 2024**, and
he **worked there in the early months of 2025** — the same months the money arrived. ⛔ **A payroll
account open in December does not close itself in January.** 🔑 **So "some payroll was run in 2025" is
not a long shot; it is the more likely reading, and it has never been tested.**

| # | Test | Strength | Depends on him? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🥇 **1** | **IRS wage & income transcript.** Shows the information returns **filed with the IRS** | ⭐ **Settles it ONE WAY** — see the bound below | ⛔ **No** — needs his IRS account **or a Form 8821** *(⚠️ **8821, not 2848** — `firm-identity.md` §4: only someone eligible to practise before the IRS may be named on a 2848, and Lilian may not be. **8821 is her form**)*. ✅ **TIMING IS NOT A CONSTRAINT** — the IRS posts the current processing year's information returns **from the first week of February**, so 2025 data has been available for **seven months**. ⓘ *An earlier draft said it "may be incomplete until well into 2026" — wrong, and it discouraged the one test that works today* |
| 🥈 **2** | **His own bank statements.** A payroll direct deposit normally carries the **provider's name** in the description *(Gusto, ADP, Paychex, QuickBooks Payroll)*; an owner draw is a plain transfer from the company account | **Strong** | Yes, but he holds them |
| 🥉 **3** | **The shape of the deposits.** Payroll is **the same amount on a regular date**; a draw is irregular. **And payroll arrives NET of withholding**, so the gross would exceed what he banked | **Good** | Yes |
| **4** | **Social Security earnings record** — `ssa.gov/myaccount` shows posted wages by year | **Strong, independent** | Yes, and he controls it. ⏰ **AND THIS ONE HAS THE LIVE DATE:** Form 4852 states that **after 30 September** following the year you may use the SSA online account to verify wages reported by your employers. **For 2025 wages that is 2026-09-30 — eight days from now** |

⛔ **BOUND ON TEST 1, AND IT IS `method.md` RULE 1b.** A transcript **hit** settles it. A **blank**
transcript establishes only that **no W-2 was filed with the IRS** — ⛔ **not that no payroll was
run.** The IRS's own caveat on that page: the transcript *"will only display information return
documents that have been filed with the IRS."* 🔑 **A locked-out shareholder's W-2 may have been
prepared and never filed** — which is **outcome ② below**, and a blank transcript is consistent with
it.

📩 **The question to put to him — facts, not documents, and answerable from his phone.**
⚠️ **IT IS DRAFTED IN ENGLISH BECAUSE HE WRITES TO US IN ENGLISH** *(his own messages, e.g. 2026-08-17)*.
🔑 **If it goes out in RUSSIAN under Lilian's name, `CLAUDE.md`'s register rule applies — simple Russian,
short sentences, active voice, no participles — and `ssa.gov`, `Gusto`, `ADP`, `Paychex` stay in English.**

```
One more thing on the money you received from Midwest Expedited in 2025.

1. Did it arrive on a regular schedule — the same amount on the same day
   each week or each month — or in irregular amounts?

2. In your bank app, what does the description on those deposits say?
   Does it name a payroll company (for example Gusto, ADP, Paychex,
   Intuit), or is it just a transfer from the company?

3. Do you have online access to your Social Security account at
   ssa.gov? If you do, it will show whether any wages were reported
   for you in 2025.
```

### 🛑 IF PART OF IT IS WAGES — WHERE IT GOES ON FORM 7203: **NOWHERE.**

⛔ **Wages never appear on Form 7203, in any line, in any part.** The form tracks **stock and debt
basis**; wages are **compensation**, they are not a K-1 item, and they do not adjust basis.
**They go on Form 1040 line 1a, from the W-2.**

🛑 **AND THE ANSWER DEPENDS ON WHICH MODEL THE RETURN IS USING — an earlier draft of this table mixed
the two, and got line 15 wrong as a result.**

**This return does NOT compute box 1 as a 50% allocated share.** Under decision 3 and §4A, **box 1 IS
the cash, used as a proxy** *(and §8 0a records that it probably overstates)*. **So the adjustment has
to stay inside that convention or the return stops being internally consistent.**

#### ✅ Under THIS return's convention — the cash proxy

**Whatever part of the cash was net wages is not a distribution AND is not box 1 income either.** Both
lines fall by the same figure, and the form's arithmetic is undisturbed.

| Form 7203 line | Direction | By how much | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| **3a** | ⬇️ **down** | the **NET** wages he banked | Under the cash proxy, box 1 is *the cash that was not wages* |
| **6** | ⬇️ **down** | the **same NET** figure | That part of the cash was never a distribution — ⚠️ **the withholding never reached him, so it is the net, not the gross** |
| **5** *(= 1+2+4)* | ⬇️ down by the same | ƒ | |
| **7** | **still -0-** | ƒ line 5 − line 6 | ✅ **The two moves cancel** |
| **15** | **still -0-** | ƒ | Line 7's skip instruction still applies |
| **1040 line 1a** | ⬆️ **up** | the **GROSS** from the W-2 | ⚠️ **Gross here, net on the 7203 — and that asymmetry is the whole trap** |
| **1040 line 25a** | ⬆️ **up** | the **withholding** | ✅ **The one element of this branch that runs in his favour** |

🔑 **And the arithmetic the asymmetry produces, stated so nobody reads it as an error:** total income
reported becomes **the cash PLUS everything deducted from the pay** — he reports *(cash − net)* as
box 1 and the **gross** on line 1a. ⚠️ **Only the FEDERAL INCOME TAX WITHHELD (W-2 box 2) comes back
as a credit on Form 1040 line 25a** — ⛔ **not the whole difference: the employee's own FICA is gone
and is not recoverable on the return** (§4D). ⓘ *The same is true of outcome ① below.*

#### ⚠️ Under a TRUE-ALLOCATION model — which we cannot use, and why it matters anyway

If box 1 were a real allocated share, **line 3a would fall by his 50% of (gross wages + the employer's
payroll taxes)** — roughly **0.54 × gross** — while **line 6 still falls by the net he banked**,
roughly **0.75 × gross**. ⛔ **The two no longer cancel**, and:
- **line 7 turns POSITIVE**, lines 8–14 stop being skipped, and **line 15 is no longer zero** — which
  breaks §4B's headline and **§7's carry row, the figure a 2026 preparer types blind**;
- **or, on the other branch, line 6 exceeds line 5** and the form's own printed Note engages:
  *"If line 6 is larger than line 5, subtract line 5 from line 6 and report the result as a capital
  gain on Form 8949 and Schedule D."*

🔑 **So "line 15 is unchanged" is TRUE ONLY BECAUSE the return uses the cash proxy — it is a
consequence of a CONVENTION, not a fact about the form.** ⛔ **The moment a real K-1 arrives, that
protection disappears and both branches above become live.**

⚠️ **And conceptually none of this is a "fall" at all.** If payroll really ran, the corporation's books
already carried the wage deduction and the true box 1 never included it. **Nothing decreases; the
invented figure has to be rebuilt from a different starting point.**

⛔ **A session that "reclassified" by moving one figure from line 6 to Form 1040 line 1a would have
the return wrong on both sides** — wrong amount on the 7203 *(net vs gross)* and an untouched box 1.

🔴 **AND A CONSEQUENCE THAT RUNS THE OTHER WAY — say it to Julia before she hopes for a W-2.** If a W-2
turns up, **the return gets MORE accurate on wages and LESS accurate on box 1.** Today the 200,053 is
being used as a proxy for his allocated share. Once part of it is known to be wages, **the remaining
cash is even further from an allocated share than it was** — and box 1 is still unknown, because there
is still no K-1. ⚠️ **§8 0a's gross-vs-share warning gets worse, not better.**
🟢 **But two things improve, and they are worth more:** wages become a **fact** instead of an estimate,
and 🔑 **a W-2 means the corporation filed 941s — which means it was still filing, which makes the 2025
Form 1120-S more likely to exist than the file currently assumes.**

### ⚖️ THE THREE OUTCOMES, AND EACH HAS A DIFFERENT FORM

⛔ **An earlier draft of this section filed Form 8919 under "the route that is NOT available" and
branched only on "no payroll." Both are wrong and are struck.** §4A had already concluded *"the route
EXISTS and had to be on the record rather than declared impossible"*, and **§4C reversed it without
noticing.** 📖 **Read off `f8919.pdf` on 2026-09-22.**

| Outcome | The form | What it costs |
|---|---|---|
| **① Payroll ran AND a W-2 was filed** | ✅ **Nothing special.** Get the W-2 *(or the transcript figures)* and report it on **Form 1040 line 1a** | **Nothing.** 🔑 **This is the outcome to hope for** |
| **② Payroll ran and NO W-2 was ever filed** | 🔴 **Form 4852**, the substitute W-2 — ⛔ **this branch did not exist in the earlier draft at all, and it is what Form 4852 is FOR.** Its **line 9** documents how the figures were derived, which is exactly the bank-deposit reasoning above | The figures are **his derivation**, not the employer's |
| **③ No payroll ran, and we still want part treated as wages** | ✅ **Form 8919 IS available** — see below | **An SS-8**, and it names the corporation |

**⛔ THE FORM 8919 DISMISSAL WAS WRONG ON ITS OWN TEXT.** *Who must file* has three conditions and
**he meets all three**: *"You performed services for a firm"* · *"You believe your pay from the firm
wasn't for services as an independent contractor"* · *"The firm didn't withhold your share of social
security and Medicare taxes from your pay."*
🔑 **And the reason codes have a catch-all written for exactly this case:** *"If none of the reason
codes apply to you, but you believe you should have been treated as an employee, **enter reason code G
and file Form SS-8** on or before the date you file your tax return."*
⛔ **Also struck: "he is a shareholder-employee who was never paid at all."** **He was paid** — he
received the money. **He was not paid AS WAGES.** That sentence is what made the dismissal read as
sound.

✅ **So the route is open. What it costs is a Form SS-8 naming the corporation** — a real consequence
in a live dispute with a partner, and a real decision. ⛔ **Julia's, on those terms — not an
impossibility.**

**⚠️ AND FORM 4852's OBJECTION WAS ALSO THE WRONG ONE.** The earlier draft said it *"requires
estimating withholding figures he does not have."* ⛔ **If no payroll ran, the withholding is ZERO —
known, not estimated.** The real objections are different and larger: **you would be attesting to
wages that were never paid**, and the form's own page carries the **§6702 frivolous-submission
penalty**.

🛑 **What is true of all three: none of them fixes the corporation's side.** The employer share, the
941s, the 940, the W-2 filing and the penalties are the **corporation's**, and ⛔ **we cannot
discharge them from his 1040 — he is locked out.** 🔑 **And §6672 means the trust-fund half can be
assessed against him personally whatever his 1040 says.**

## 4D · 🔑 SALARY vs DISTRIBUTION vs K-1 INCOME — what each one IS, and where each one LANDS

> 🟠 **PARTLY SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23 — it answers Lilian's question, which is why it stays whole.**
> ✅ **The TEACHING is unaffected:** what a salary is, what a distribution is, what box 1 is, and that
> **box 1 and box 16D are not alternatives** — that is true of any S corporation and is the point of
> the section.
> ⛔ **What is dead is the SCORING:** scenarios ①–⑤ all describe **this** return under the §4B route,
> and **none of them is what gets filed.** Julia's route is **a sixth shape the section does not
> work** — a Schedule C, with ①'s capital-gain branch gone and ②'s wage branch replaced by
> self-employment income. 🔑 **§4E scores the live one.**

_(Lilian's question, 2026-09-22: **"si todo el dinero sacado fue distribución, entiendo que va en la
casilla 16D del K1 input, pero si fuera salario, ¿a dónde va este número?… ¿y qué tal si hay parte
salario y parte distribuciones?"** — written as the teaching section, because the same confusion
produces two different wrong returns.)_

### 🛑 THE ONE THING THAT MAKES THIS CONFUSING: box 1 and box 16D ARE NOT ALTERNATIVES

**They answer two different questions about two different things, and a real K-1 carries both:**

| | What it records | It is… |
|---|---|---|
| **Box 1** — ordinary business income | What the corporation **EARNED** and allocated to him | **INCOME.** He is taxed on it whether or not he took a cent out |
| **Box 16D** — distributions | What the corporation **PAID OUT** to him in cash | ⛔ **NOT INCOME.** It is him taking money that is already his — ⚠️ *true here because the corporation has **no accumulated E&P**; with AE&P a distribution above AAA would be a **dividend** under §1368(c). Settled for this client at §8 item 2* |

⛔ **So "report it all as a distribution" is not ONE choice — it is TWO**: what goes in box 1, **and**
what goes in box 16D. 🔑 **And if you put the money in 16D while leaving box 1 at zero, the return
says the corporation earned him nothing and handed him 200,053 anyway — which is a CAPITAL GAIN.**
⚠️ **That is the trap this section exists for, and it is the literal reading of "todo como
distribución."**

### 📍 WHERE EACH ONE GOES — the address, and the software route

📎 **Read on 2026-09-22, each off its own current PDF on irs.gov:** `f1040.pdf`, `f1040s1.pdf`,
`f1040se.pdf`, `f8949.pdf`, `f1040sd.pdf`, `i1040sd.pdf` and `i2210.pdf` — **2025 editions** — and
`f7203.pdf` with `i7203.pdf`, which are **Rev. December 2022** and still the current edition *(§3A)*.
⚠️ **The 2025 Form 1040 renumbered:** the capital-gain line is **7a** *(7b is a checkbox row, not a
money line)*, wages are **1a**, withholding is **25a**, and the QBI deduction is **13a**.
🛠️ **The ATX screens named below are established, not inferred** — `W2 (1040)` from the input screen
itself *(Valentin Volzhanskiy's paper, 2026-09-13)*, `K1 INPUT (1120S)` → `Ordinary income (loss)`
from Artem Markarian's, and the **box 16D** field from Lilian at the keyboard, 2026-09-22 (§3A).

| | **SALARY (W-2)** | **K-1 BOX 1 income** | **BOX 16D distribution** |
|---|---|---|---|
| 📄 **Source document** | a **Form W-2** | Schedule K-1 box 1 | Schedule K-1 box 16, code D |
| 🛠️ **Typed in ATX** | the **`W2 (1040)`** form — ⛔ **nothing to do with the K-1 screen** | `K1 INPUT (1120S)` → `Ordinary income (loss)` | `K1 INPUT (1120S)` → the **box 16D** field |
| 📍 **Lands on Form 1040** | **line 1a** *(→ 1z)* | **line 8**, via Schedule E page 2 col (k) → Schedule 1 line 5 → Schedule 1 line 10 | ⛔ **NOWHERE** — unless it exceeds basis, then **line 7a** via Form 8949 → Schedule D |
| 🧮 **Form 7203** | ⛔ **does not appear at all** | **line 3a** — ⬆️ **increases** basis | **line 6** — ⬇️ **decreases** basis |
| 💰 **Income tax** | ✅ ordinary | ✅ ordinary | ⛔ none, within basis |
| 🏥 **FICA / SE tax** | ✅ **BOTH halves** — his withheld, the company's paid | ⛔ **none** | ⛔ **none** |
| 🧾 **Withholding credit** | ✅ **Form 1040 line 25a** | — | — |
| 📊 **§199A / QBI** | ⛔ **does NOT count** as QBI *(§199A(c)(4)(A) excludes reasonable compensation paid to the taxpayer)* — ⓘ *the corporation's W-2 wages DO feed the §199A(b)(2)(B) wage limitation, which can RAISE a deduction; moot here, taxable income is under the threshold (§3C)* | ✅ counts | — |

🔑 **Read the FICA row and the §199A row together and you have the whole reason this question is
worth asking.** **Salary carries an employment-tax cost the other two do not** — ⛔ **and it is NOT a
flat 15.3%, which §4A already struck once:** social security is **12.4% only up to the $176,100 wage
base** and Medicare is **2.9% with no ceiling**, so on a gross near 200,053 the combined employer and
employee cost is about **27,600 — roughly 13.8%**, and the rate keeps falling as the gross rises.
✅ **Salary is also the only one that brings a withholding credit back**, and ⛔ **the only one that is
not QBI.**

### 🧮 THE FIVE SCENARIOS, WORKED — same 200,053, five different returns

**Opening basis is ZERO in every one of them (§2F-i).**

#### ① 🔴 ALL DISTRIBUTION, nothing else — *the literal reading of the question, and the only one that creates a capital gain*

| Form · line | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Form 7203 line 3a | **0** | nothing reported as income |
| Form 7203 line 5 *(basis before distributions)* | **0** | ƒ 0 + 0 + 0 |
| Form 7203 line 6 | **200,053** | the distribution |
| 🔴 **The excess** | **200,053** | 📎 the form's own Note: *"If line 6 is larger than line 5, subtract line 5 from line 6 and report the result as a capital gain on Form 8949 and Schedule D."* |
| **Form 1040 line 7a** | **200,053 capital gain** | via Form 8949 → Schedule D |
| Form 7203 line 7 · line 15 | **-0-** | ⛔ **and basis is NOT increased by the gain** |
| Form 1040 line 1a | 0 | no W-2 |

⚠️ **Long-term or short-term follows the HOLDING PERIOD of the stock**, not the distribution —
📎 §1368(b)(2) deems it gain from a **sale or exchange of the stock**. ⛔ **Nobody has established his
acquisition date** *(it is Form 7203 item D, §8 item 6b)*, so the character is open.
🗺️ **The route, addressed:** **Form 8949 Part I box C** *(short-term)* **or Part II box F**
*(long-term)*, amount in **column (h)** → **Schedule D line 3** *(box C)* **or line 10** *(box F)* →
line 16 → **Form 1040 line 7a**.
⛔ **NOT lines 1b or 8b — an earlier draft said so and it was wrong.** 📎 Read off `f1040sd.pdf`:
line **1b** is *"Box A or Box G"*, line **8b** is *"Box D or Box J"* — **the BROKER-REPORTED rows.**
🔑 **And that is exactly what a §1368(b)(2) gain is not:** it carries **no Form 1099-B**, which is why
box C or box F is ticked in the first place — so the Schedule D line has to be **3 or 10.** *(Form
8949's own face prints the mapping.)*
⚠️ **And the Form 8082 changes with it:** column (c) and (d) would both be **zero** for box 1 income —
⛔ **so the item described in column (a) would have to be the DISTRIBUTION, not the income**, or the
form discloses nothing at all (§3B, §5A).

> 🛑 **AN EARLIER DRAFT CALLED THIS "THE EXPENSIVE ONE". THAT WAS ASSERTED, NEVER TESTED, AND ON 2025
> FEDERAL TAX IT IS BACKWARDS.** ⚖️ **Run against ④, which is identical in every other column:**
> both start from taxable income before the NOL of **168,553** *(200,053 − the 31,500 MFJ standard
> deduction)*; both absorb the same **134,842** of NOL *(the §172 80% limit, computed without the
> NOL)*; both leave **33,711**. ⛔ **But in ④ that residue is ORDINARY and in ① it is CAPITAL GAIN** —
> and if the gain is long-term, the **2025 MFJ 0% rate runs to $96,700** *(📎 Schedule D Tax
> Worksheet line 15, `i1040sd.pdf` 2025)*. So ① comes out roughly three and a half thousand dollars
> cheaper — **not more expensive.**
> ⚠️ **THAT FIGURE IS ILLUSTRATIVE, NOT A COMPUTED RESULT FOR THIS HOUSEHOLD.** It assumes **200,053
> is their ENTIRE 2025 income** — ⛔ and the 2024 return carried a **spouse's Schedule C** (§6), while
> §8 item 4 leaves open whether there is a 2025 Schedule C at all. **Do not quote the number to
> Julia as a calculation.**
> ⛔ **AND IT IS NOT A REASON TO CHOOSE ①** — which is the whole point of this section's closing.
> 🔑 **It is recorded for the opposite reason: so nobody rejects ① believing it costs more, and so
> nobody is surprised later.** ⚠️ *The §199A leg of the old justification was moot too — §3C has the
> 2024 carryforward driving the deduction to nil in **every** scenario.*
> ⚠️ **Two things that could reverse it, neither established:** a **short-term** holding period taxes
> the gain at ordinary rates and the two become equal; and the NOL is consumed identically either
> way, so ① spends 134,842 of carryforward sheltering income the 0% bracket would have covered for
> nothing. 📌 **If this ever becomes a live choice, compute it — do not reuse this paragraph.**

#### ② ALL SALARY — *no K-1 figures at all*

| Form · line | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Form 1040 line 1a** | the **GROSS** from the W-2 | ⚠️ **NOT 200,053 if that is what he BANKED** — a pay deposit is net of withholding, so the gross is **higher** |
| **Form 1040 line 25a** | the federal withholding | ✅ comes back as a credit |
| Form 7203 lines 3a · 6 | **0 · 0** | ⛔ **salary touches neither** |
| Form 7203 line 15 | **-0-** | basis never moved |
| Schedule E page 2 | **nothing from this corporation** | |

🔑 **AND FORM 7203 IS PROBABLY NOT FILED AT ALL** — no loss claimed, no distribution, no disposal, no
loan repayment (§4B item 3). ✅ **FICA is already settled** through payroll, both halves.

#### ③ ALL K-1 INCOME, no distribution — *this is §4B's variant B*

| Form · line | Value |
|---|---|
| Schedule E p2 Part II **col (k)** → Schedule 1 line 5 → **Form 1040 line 8** | **200,053** ordinary |
| Form 7203 line 3a → line 5 | **200,053** |
| Form 7203 line 6 | **0** |
| Form 7203 **line 15** | 🔴 **200,053 — carried into 2026** |

⛔ **Says he earned 200,053 and took nothing out**, which contradicts the one fact we hold.
⚠️ **AND ON THIS BRANCH FORM 7203 IS NOT FILED EITHER** — §4B item 3: no loss, no distribution, no
disposal, no loan repayment, so none of the four triggers fires. 🔑 **The line values above are what
the form WOULD say; §7 still carries the 200,053 forward, because the basis is real whether or not a
form states it.** ⓘ *`i7203.pdf` recommends completing and retaining it anyway, for exactly this
reason.*

#### ④ ⛔ BOTH — *~~what §4B variant A files, and what the worksheet is built on~~* — **NEITHER IS TRUE NOW:** variant A is moot (§4B), and **the ATX worksheet is built on the Schedule C** (§4F)

| Form · line | Value |
|---|---|
| Schedule E p2 col (k) → **Form 1040 line 8** | **200,053** ordinary |
| Form 7203 line 3a → line 5 | **200,053** |
| Form 7203 line 6 | **200,053** |
| Form 7203 line 7 → **line 15** | **-0-** |
| **Capital gain** | ⛔ **NONE** — the income raised basis *before* the distribution reduced it (§1367's order) |

⚠️ **THAT "NONE" IS CONDITIONAL, and §4B item 1 exists because an earlier version stated it flatly.**
It holds only while **2025 is an income year** *(a chosen reporting figure, not a fact)* and only
while **debt basis really is zero** *(if he did lend, Reg. §1.1367-2(c) restores the DEBT first,
stock basis stays at zero, and the distribution becomes taxable)*. **Read §4B item 1 with this row.**

#### ⑤ MIXED — part salary, part distribution

**Let `G` = the W-2 gross, `N` = what he actually banked from payroll.** The rest of the cash,
**200,053 − N**, is the distribution.

| Form · line | Value |
|---|---|
| **Form 1040 line 1a** | **G** *(the GROSS — W-2 box 1)* |
| **Form 1040 line 25a** | 🛑 **W-2 box 2 ONLY** — ⛔ **NOT `G − N`.** See the red block below |
| Form 7203 **line 6** | **200,053 − N** — ⚠️ **the NET comes out, because the withholding never reached him** |
| Form 7203 **line 3a** | **200,053 − N** — under this return's cash-proxy convention (§4C) |
| Form 7203 line 7 → line 15 | **-0-** — ✅ the two moves cancel |

🔑 **Total income reported becomes the cash PLUS everything that was deducted from the pay** — he
reports *(200,053 − N)* as box 1 and **G** on line 1a, so the total is **200,053 + (G − N)**.

### 🛑 `G − N` IS NOT THE WITHHOLDING, AND TYPING IT ON LINE 25a OVERSTATES HIS PAYMENTS

⛔ **An earlier draft of this section wrote `G − N` into the line 25a row. That is wrong and is
struck.** `G − N` is **everything** that came out of the gross:

| What came out of the gross | Recoverable on the 1040? |
|---|---|
| **Federal income tax withheld** — W-2 **box 2** | ✅ **YES — this, and only this, is line 25a** *(the form says "Federal income tax withheld from: a Form(s) W-2")* |
| **Employee social security** — box 4 *(6.2% to the $176,100 wage base)* | ⛔ **NO. It is gone.** ⓘ *The §31(b) excess-social-security credit needs **two or more employers**; one employer over-withholding is corrected by the employer, not on the return* |
| **Employee Medicare** — box 6 *(1.45%, no ceiling)* | ⛔ **NO** |
| **State withholding, and anything else on the stub** | ⛔ **not line 25a** |

⚠️ **The size of the error, so nobody treats it as a rounding point:** on a gross near 200,053 the
employee's own FICA alone is roughly **13,800** — 6.2% of the wage base plus 1.45% of the whole.
**Putting `G − N` on line 25a would claim that as a payment he never made.**
✅ **What IS in his favour on this branch is narrower: the box 2 withholding, which he has already
paid and which comes back as a credit.** 🔑 **And one thing the table cannot show: that withholding
is treated as paid RATABLY THROUGH THE YEAR** — *"you are considered to have paid one-fourth of these
amounts on each payment due date"* *(📎 2025 Form 2210 instructions)* — so a salary branch helps
against the estimated-tax penalty §4A raises. ⚠️ **But do not read that as the deciding factor: the
no-tax-liability exception may waive §6654 in every branch — see the note under the comparison
table.**
📌 **You need the W-2 itself, not the bank deposit, to fill any of this in** — the deposit gives you
`N` and nothing else.

#### ⑥ ⛔ AND A SIXTH THAT IS NOT ON THE TABLE BECAUSE IT IS NOT A CHARACTER OF THIS MONEY AT ALL

🔴 **THE LOAN DRAW.** §4A's fifth character and §8 item 0B: **the corporation was carrying very large
loans TO its shareholders**, so the cash he received **may be a draw against a shareholder loan — the
CASH would then be neither income nor a distribution**, and a loan advance is a balance-sheet
movement rather than a 1040 line.

⛔ **BUT "IT IS NOT ON HIS 1040 AT ALL" IS TOO CLEAN, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT SAID IT. THREE THINGS PUT
IT BACK:**

| | Why it lands on the return anyway |
|---|---|
| **§7872 — below-market loan** | 📖 **Pub. 550 lists *corporation-shareholder loans* among the covered categories by name**, and the **forgone interest** is deemed transferred from the corporation to the shareholder each year. ⛔ **The de minimis exception caps at $10,000 of total loans between the two — against 994,031 falling to 34,213 on Schedule L (§2F-iii), it is not in play.** ⚠️ **READ THE AMOUNT BEFORE THE CHARACTER: it is the FORGONE INTEREST — *"interest that would be payable … at the applicable federal rate … minus any interest actually payable"* — NOT the principal.** ⛔ **This is a few thousand dollars, not a second 200,053.** 🔑 **AND ITS CHARACTER IS NOT SETTLED. Pub. 550 in terms:** *"The nature of the forgone interest … **depends on the relationship between lender and borrower. It may be considered a gift, an investment, compensation, etc.**"* **Two branches are live here and they land in different places:** ⓐ a **corporation-shareholder** reading makes it a distribution — *and then, **if** stock basis is zero, a §1368(b)(2) gain, which is **reasoning from §1368's ordering, not read in a source*** ⓑ ⚠️ **a COMPENSATION-RELATED reading — *"any below-market loan between an employer and an employee"* — makes it WAGES**, with FICA, which is scenario ②'s shape. 🔴 **He is a shareholder-EMPLOYEE recorded at 100% of his time (§2F-ii), and §4C's whole subject is whether this money was compensation — so ⓑ is not the remote branch here** |
| **Forgiveness** | ⛔ **§8 item 0B — the item this scenario cites — already asks it in terms: *"Repaid? Forgiven — which is income?"*** A forgiven draw lands on the return. **"Not on his 1040" is the flat version of a question this file has already marked OPEN** |
| **Box 1 is not zero — it is UNMEASURED** | 🔑 **The corporation still allocates him a share of 2025 ordinary income whatever the cash was.** ⛔ **What the loan-draw reading destroys is the PROXY, not box 1's existence** (§4A's health warning). **A reader could otherwise conclude the return reports nothing at all from this corporation, which is a different and wrong conclusion.** ⚠️ **AND IT CUTS BACK AGAINST ROW ONE:** the §1368(b)(2) branch there presumes stock basis is zero — **but any positive box 1 raises basis under §1367 BEFORE §1368 reduces it**, and would absorb a forgone-interest-sized deemed distribution entirely. 🔑 **The two rows only both stand while box 1 really is nil, which is precisely what nobody knows** |

⛔ **It is excluded from the five scenarios by construction, not by evidence** — §4B item 2 made
exactly this criticism of its own two-row table, and a five-row table earns the same one.
🔑 **Nobody can currently rule it out**, and it is the one reading under which this return reports
the wrong thing entirely rather than the right thing imprecisely.

### ⚖️ THE COMPARISON IN ONE LINE EACH

| | Ordinary income on 1040 | Capital gain | FICA | Line 25a credit | §6654 estimated-tax penalty | 2026 opening basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ① all distribution | **0** | 🔴 **200,053** | none | none | 🟠 **see below** | **-0-** |
| ② all salary | **the gross** | none | ✅ **both halves** | ✅ **W-2 box 2 only** | 🟢 **helps** — withholding is deemed paid ratably | **-0-** |
| ③ all K-1 income | **200,053** | none | none | none | 🟠 **see below** | 🔴 **200,053** |
| ④ **both — what we file** | **200,053** | none | none | none | 🟠 **see below** | **-0-** |
| ⑤ mixed | **the gross + (200,053 − N)** | none | ✅ on the salary part | ✅ **box 2 only** | 🟢 **helps in part** | **-0-** |

> 🛑 **THE §6654 COLUMN, AND WHY IT SAYS "SEE BELOW" RATHER THAN "NOT CURED".** ⛔ **An earlier draft
> marked ①③④ "not cured", which asserts a negative about a test nobody has run** — `method.md` rule 1b.
> ✅ **What IS verified** *(📎 2025 Instructions for Form 2210, read 2026-09-22)*: withholding is
> ratable — *"For withheld federal income tax … you are considered to have paid **one-fourth** of
> these amounts on each payment due date unless you can show otherwise"* — so the salary branches do
> help. 🔑 **But the REAL discriminator is probably not withholding at all. It is 2024:**
> *"You won't have to pay the penalty or file this form if … **you had no tax liability for 2024**,
> you were a U.S. citizen or resident alien for the entire year, and your 2024 tax return was … for a
> full 12 months."*
> 🟢 **CLOSED 2026-09-23 — §4J-ii. THE ANSWER IS YES, AND THE PENALTY IS ZERO IN ALL FIVE BRANCHES.**
> **Both blockers named below are settled** *(the spouse's 2024 Schedule C nets 131, under the 400 SE
> floor, with Schedule 2 line 4 blank; and the unread page-1 lines cannot rescue it, because the test
> is "was there tax SHOWN", and the 2024 refundable credits exceed the tax by 1,830)*. ⛔ **The 656 on
> the filed return comes off in full.** ⚠️ **One limb left to confirm: 12 full months and U.S.
> residence throughout.** ⓘ *Everything below stood as written and is kept for the trail.*
>
> **2024 taxable income was (537,926)** (§5A). ⛔ **If the 2024 tax was zero, the penalty is waived in
> ALL FIVE branches and this column is moot.** ⚠️ **Two things stop that being settled, and both are
> already in this file:** the **spouse's Schedule C** *(SE tax would be a 2024 tax liability — §6
> records its header on one of the partially-extracted pages)*, and §6's record that 1040 page 1's
> **lines 2b, 3b, 4b, 6b and 7 were never read.** 📌 **So: establish 2024's tax liability before
> costing §6654 in any branch.** ⓘ *A second route lands in the same place: the prior-year safe
> harbour is **100%** of 2024's tax — not 110%, since 2024 AGI was far below the $150,000 trigger — so
> a zero 2024 tax is satisfied by any payment at all.*

⛔ **THE TABLE HAS NO TAX COLUMN, DELIBERATELY** — see scenario ①: the one comparison that was run
came out the opposite way from the framing an earlier draft assumed, and a cost ranking across five
branches would have to be computed, not tabulated. 📌 **Three things it also does not show, none of
them settled here:** **NIIT** *(almost certainly moot — AGI after the NOL is far below the 250,000
MFJ threshold — but ①'s gain is a distribution IN EXCESS of basis, which §4B's within-basis
reasoning does not reach)*; **Illinois and any other state**, where wages, K-1 income and capital
gain source differently (§4A); and **§199A**, which §3C expects to be nil in every branch.

### ⚖️ SO WHICH OF THESE IS IT? — the honest answer, and it is not "whichever we prefer"

✅ **The characterisation is mostly a FACT about what the corporation did**, and §4C's point is that
the fact can be **tested** — the wage & income transcript, his bank descriptions, his `ssa.gov`
record — rather than picked.
⚠️ **But "fact, not choice" overstates it, and this paper's own pages say so in two places:**
- **§4B is a genuine choice** — whether to record a distribution alongside the income when there is
  no K-1 to state either. **Two material figures on this return are chosen rather than measured**
  (§4C).
- ⛔ **And §4C outcome ③ is a second one:** if no payroll ran, **Form 8919 is available**, through
  reason code G and a Form SS-8. **That is characterisation offered as a decision — Julia's, on the
  terms §4C sets out.**

🔑 **The accurate line: go and establish the fact first, because most of this is discoverable and
none of it should be guessed. What is left after that is a short list of real decisions, and they
belong to the signer.**

## 4E · 🔴 THE SCHEDULE C ROUTE — Julia's reversal of 2026-09-23, and what it moves

_(Lilian, 2026-09-23: **"Hablé con Julia y me dijo que son las estrategias que vamos a seguir. Vamos a
incluir ese income de $200,000 en un Schedule C, junto con los gastos que reportó el cliente… Vamos a
reportar cero salario y cero distribuciones… vamos a someter la Forma 8082… no quiero reportar ningún
income por parte de esas tres compañías porque no tenemos la información."**)_

> ⛔ **THIS REVERSES DECISION 3 AND TAKES THE ROUTE §4A NAMED AS THE ONE THAT MUST NOT BE TAKEN.**
> §4A's first table is headed *"the route that must NOT be taken — Schedule C"*, for two reasons.
> 🔑 **It is Julia's call and it is now the plan** — but she is entitled to have the two reasons
> restated, because one of them is a number.

### 🛑 THE OVERRULED-OBJECTION RECORD — written because the decision is the signer's and the objection is the preparer's

🔑 **This paper follows [`form-1120s-preparation`](../../sops/form-1120s-preparation.md) §5C-v's rule
for a directed treatment whose gates fail: the direction is carried out, and a note records what was
done and how to undo it.** ⛔ **A directed return with no note is the failure that rule exists to
prevent** — the figures reconcile to nothing and nobody remembers why.

| | |
|---|---|
| **The direction** | Report the 200,053 and the client's expenses on a **Schedule C**; zero salary; zero distributions; a **Form 8082 for each of the three corporations** |
| **Who, when, how** | **Julia**, relayed by **Lilian**, **2026-09-23**. ⚠️ **Relayed, not heard directly** — the wording in the quotation above is Lilian's account of the conversation |
| 🛑 **The objection, which was NOT withdrawn** | **(1)** 📎 Schedule SE excludes income received *"for performing services as an employee"*, and a shareholder-officer serving his own S corporation **is** an employee for employment-tax purposes — **so this money cannot be sole-proprietor gross receipts** (§4A, §4E). **(2)** It costs roughly **17,000–27,000** in SE tax *(≈ 16,841 – 27,194 — §4E's banner; an earlier "19,000–27,000" here was stale at the bottom)*, against **≈13,819** on **Form 8919**, which fits the fact pattern and was already on the record (§4C) |
| **What the objection would have produced instead** | ⚖️ **Three routes were live:** §4B variant A *(box 1 income plus a distribution)*, **Form 8919 + Form SS-8** *(if the money is wages the corporation never ran)*, or **Form 4852** *(if payroll ran and no W-2 was filed — §4C outcome ②)*. 🔑 **Which one is right depends on the payroll answer, which is still unobtained** |
| 🔓 **HOW TO UNDO IT** | **Delete the Schedule C, Schedule SE and the Schedule 1 lines 3, 15 and 17 entries; restore the K-1 input; set Form 8082 column (d) back to 200,053 on the Midwest form only and withdraw the other two.** ⚠️ **After filing it is a Form 1040-X**, and 🔑 **the SE tax paid is not lost — it is refundable on the amendment**, which is why this is reversible at all |
| ⛔ **What it is NOT** | **This is not a defect finding and Julia must not be handed it as one.** [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) **§4C:** many decisions are the reviewer's own, and one handed back with its date checks in seconds. 🔑 **It is a DECISION with a recorded objection — she confirms it or overturns it** |

#### ⚖️ AND THE FIRM'S OWN POSITION, because the objection is about correctness and not only about cost

⚠️ **§4C already sets out the firm's obligations and they are not superseded — they sharpen.**
📎 **Circular 230 §10.34(c)**: a practitioner must advise the client of any penalty reasonably likely
to apply and of the opportunity to avoid it by **disclosure**. 📎 **§10.21**: known non-compliance must
be brought to the client's attention. ⚠️ **§6694** reaches the preparer where a position lacks
substantial authority and is not disclosed.

🔑 **What that means here, stated plainly and without alarm:**

1. ✅ **Disclosure is already on this return.** The Midwest Form 8082's Part III says the funds were
   received **for services he performed** and that they are reported on Schedule C. ⛔ **That sentence
   is not decoration — it is what converts an undisclosed position into a disclosed one.** 🛑 **So it
   must not be softened or dropped to make the two filings look more consistent.**
2. 🛑 **The payroll question gates transmission** (§9A ④). ⛔ **If a W-2 was filed, the Schedule C is
   wrong on its face and no disclosure repairs it.**
3. 📌 **DECLINING TO SIGN BELONGS IN JULIA'S OPTION SET, and it is named here because leaving it out
   would be the omission.** ⛔ **Nothing in this paper says the firm should decline** — the position is
   disclosed, the client's situation is genuine, and the route is Julia's to choose as the EA who
   signs. ✅ **It is listed so that the option set she is choosing from is complete: confirm · overturn
   to one of the three alternatives · obtain the payroll answer first · or decline.**

> not self-employment income either.** ⛔ **So there is no state of the world in which this 200,053
> is sole-proprietor gross receipts.** It is **wages**, a **distribution**, or a **loan draw** — the
> same three §9A ② lists.

> a federal provision on **interstate motor-carrier employees** *(49 U.S.C. §14503)* is generally
> understood to stop any state but the employee's residence taxing their compensation — 🔑 **and it
> reaches EMPLOYEES, not self-employed owner-operators.** ⚠️ **If that holds, the Schedule C may
> CREATE a multi-state sourcing problem the wage route would have removed** — which bears directly on
> the Illinois question in §4A. ⛔ **NOT asserted: nobody has read the statute.** 📌 **Check it before
> Julia prices the decision; do not write it into the return as authority until someone has.**
> 🔴 **AND ONE CONSEQUENCE THAT HAS NO HOME ANYWHERE ELSE: there IS real income tax on this return,
> on income that had NO WITHHOLDING.** 📎 **§6654** — an underpayment-of-estimated-tax exposure
> §4A flagged for the old route and nobody has costed for this one. ⚠️ **The prior-year exception may
> save it** *(no tax liability in 2024)*, **which nobody has checked.**
>
> 🟢 **ONE THING THAT IS NOT TRIGGERED, checked rather than assumed:** the **Additional Medicare Tax**
> (0.9%, Form 8959) applies above **250,000 for a joint return** on combined wages and self-employment
> income. **Net earnings here top out around 184,749**, so it does not fire on his side alone.
> ⚠️ **It would if the spouse has substantial earned income — which this paper does not establish**
> (§8 item 4d). 📌 **Not asserted as closed.**

### ✅ HER TWO QUESTIONS — one was answered correctly, and one was answered WRONG

> 🛑 **CORRECTION, 2026-09-23 — I gave Lilian the wrong answer to her first question and it is
> restated here rather than quietly edited.** ⛔ **I told her Part II is left blank. It is not.**
> 📎 **`i8082.pdf`, the FIRST Note under the heading *Specific Instructions*, verbatim:**
>
> > *"If the pass-through entity didn't file a return or give you a Schedule K-1, Schedule K-3 (and
> > the pass-through entity was required to provide one to you according to the instructions for
> > Schedule K-2), Schedule Q, and/or foreign trust statement by the time you're required to file
> > your return, **complete Parts I and II to the best of your knowledge.**"*
>
> 🔑 **That Note governs EXACTLY this fact pattern** — no K-1 by the filing date — **and it is an
> instruction to complete Part II, not to skip it.** ⚠️ **§3B of this same paper already had it
> right** (column (c) = **zero**, both column (b) boxes); **§4F-iv reversed it silently, and that is
> the defect.**

#### ⚖️ The two instructions, and how they actually fit together

📎 **`i8082.pdf`, "How Many Forms To Complete":**

> *"You must complete and file a **separate form for each pass-through entity** for which you're
> reporting an inconsistent or AAR item. … **You don't need to complete lines 8 through 11 if not
> reporting a change to the amount or treatment of a monetary item; however, you must include an
> explanation of the change(s) in Part III.**"*

📎 **And the Note at the head of *Lines 8 Through 11—Inconsistent Treatment*:**

> *"Lines 8 through 11 are only required if reporting a change to the amount or treatment of a
> monetary number."*

🛑 **CORRECTED 2026-09-24 — THE CONCLUSION STANDS, BUT IT WAS RESTING ON THE WRONG NOTE.** ⛔ ~~The
*Specific Instructions* Note is written for exactly one fact pattern — no K-1 by the filing date —
which is ours~~. 🔑 **The Note quoted just above says nothing about a filing date.** ✅ **There is a
SECOND Note, and THAT one is written for exactly our facts** — it sits at the head of the *Specific
Instructions*, ahead of *Inconsistent Treatment*:

> *"**Note.** If the pass-through entity didn't file a return or give you a Schedule K-1, Schedule K-3
> … Schedule Q, and/or foreign trust statement **by the time you're required to file your return,
> complete Parts I and II to the best of your knowledge**."*

🔑 **Line 8 IS Part II.** 📌 **So the tie-breaker is still SPECIFICITY and the answer is still the same:
the general waiver bows to a Note addressed to this exact fact pattern, and LINE 8 IS COMPLETED ON ALL
THREE FORMS.** ⚠️ **And completing line 8 with zeros cannot make the return wrong**, while leaving it
blank against a Note that says to complete it can.
ⓘ *An earlier version of this passage argued instead that "the instructions themselves treat a
missing K-1 as a change of TREATMENT", from column (b)'s tick rule. ⛔ **That reasoning is struck as
circular** — column (b) tells you which boxes to tick WHEN completing line 8; it does not settle
whether line 8 is required. ✅ **The conclusion is unchanged, and now rests on the Note alone.***

| Her question | Answer |
|---|---|
| *"¿Es compatible someter la Forma 8082 sin poner ningún monto en el K-1 input?"* | ✅ **YES — the two are compatible, and that part of my answer stands.** **Nothing is typed into the ATX K-1 input**, and nothing from these three corporations appears anywhere on the return. 🛑 **But that is NOT the same as leaving Part II blank on the form.** **Line 8 is completed: column (a) description · column (b) BOTH boxes · column (c) ZERO · column (d) ZERO · column (e) ZERO.** 🔑 **Column (d) is zero *because* nothing is being reported from the entity — the zero is the disclosure, and a blank line 8 would not say it** |
| *"¿Tengo que hacer una Forma 8082 por cada uno de los K-1 que no recibí?"* | ✅ **YES — one per pass-through entity. THREE corporations, THREE Forms 8082.** ⛔ **The form cannot name more than one entity: lines 3, 4, 5 and 6 are all singular.** ✅ **This answer was right and is unchanged** |

#### 🔴 AND A THIRD THING SHE DID NOT ASK, WHICH CHANGES WHAT THESE FORMS *ARE*

📎 **`i8082.pdf`, *Who Must File*, the bullet this paper has been relying on, verbatim:**

> *"The pass-through entity hasn't filed a tax return or given you a Schedule K-1, Schedule Q, or
> foreign trust statement by the time you're required to file your tax return (including extensions)
> **and there are items you must include on your return.**"*

🛑 **Under Julia's route there are NO items from any of the three on this return** — zero salary, zero
distributions, nothing on Schedule E. ⛔ **So the mandatory trigger is not met, and the forms become a
PROTECTIVE DISCLOSURE rather than a required notice.**
⚠️ **THAT IS FIRM ON THE TWO SILENT CORPORATIONS AND ARGUABLE ON MIDWEST**, and the difference is
stated rather than smoothed over: **the Schedule C money IS Midwest's money** — §4E's own closing
says so and the Midwest Part III tells the IRS so — **so "items you must include on your return" is
at least arguably satisfied for that one.** ✅ **It changes nothing in practice, because the form is
being filed either way** 🔑 **— but if anyone asks whether we were REQUIRED to file it, the honest
answer for Midwest is "probably, and we did not rely on that."**
⚠️ **§3B is headed *"Why it is compulsory"*. That heading was written for the route where 200,053 went
on Schedule E as an estimated item, and it does NOT survive the reversal** — §3B is marked
accordingly.

✅ **Filing them anyway is still the right call, and the instructions say what it buys** — 📎 the
*Caution* under *Who Must File*: *"If you don't notify the IRS that you're reporting an item (box (a)
of Part I, line 1) inconsistently, any deficiency … **may be assessed immediately**"* — i.e. without
the ordinary deficiency procedures. 🔑 **We do not know what those three corporations filed. If a
K-1 surfaces later showing income, the protective notice is what preserves his position.**
📌 **Julia's to confirm, because filing a form nobody requires is a disclosure choice, not a
compliance step.**

🛠️ ⚠️ **Whether ATX will let you add three copies of Form 8082 to one return is a SCREEN fact nobody
here has seen** *(part 8d)*. ⛔ **Not asserted.** 📌 **If it will not, the fallback is a PDF
attachment — check on screen and tell me, and it gets recorded.**

### 🛑 AND THE ONE THING THAT MUST NOT BE FILED AS-IS: the Midwest 8082 now contradicts the Schedule C

⛔ **The money on the Schedule C IS Midwest Expedited's money.** So a Form 8082 for Midwest saying
*"no K-1 received"* and reporting **nothing** from that entity, filed alongside a Schedule C carrying
**200,053 of that corporation's money**, is two filings describing the same cash two different ways
**with nothing on the return connecting them.**

✅ **The fix is in Part III, and it costs one paragraph.** 🔑 **The Midwest form's explanation must say
what was done with the money; the other two say only that nothing was received.** ⛔ **Do not file
three identical forms.**

#### 🔴 LINE 24b — THE MEALS RATE IS A GATE QUESTION, AND ON THESE FACTS IT IS PROBABLY **80%**, NOT 50%

📎 **2025 Instructions for Schedule C, "Amount of deduction", verbatim:**

> *"In most cases, you can deduct only **50%** of your business meal expenses… However, for
> individuals subject to the **Department of Transportation (DOT) hours of service limits**, the
> percentage is increased to **80%** for business meals consumed during or incident to any period of
> duty for which those limits are in effect. Individuals subject to the DOT hours of service limits
> include the following. … • **Interstate truck operators who are under DOT regulations.**"*

🛑 **This client's activity is trucking** — the corporation is **Midwest *Expedited* Corp**, the
expense list contains **truck repair** and **towing**, and the travel line is *"work trips — gas,
hotels, food."* ⛔ **An earlier version of this section wrote a flat 50% and that was wrong to assert.**

| The question, and it must be put to the client | What it decides |
|---|---|
| 🔑 **Was HE personally driving under DOT hours-of-service limits during 2025** — an interstate truck operator — or was he managing the operation and dispatching? | 🟢 **Driving under DOT limits → 80%** on the meals consumed during or incident to those periods. 🟠 **Managing, not driving → 50%.** ⚠️ **Mixed → each meal follows the duty period it belonged to** |

📌 **Ask it in the same message as the 1099 questions.** ⛔ **Do not default to 80% because it is
bigger, and do not default to 50% because it is safer — it is a fact about what he did, and he knows
it.** ⚠️ **On the 2,378 "trip" row the period is not even stated (§4E), so that one needs the purpose
first and the rate second.**

#### 🔴 LINE 9 — IT CANNOT BE TYPED ALONE: SCHEDULE C **PART IV** OR **FORM 4562** IS REQUIRED

📎 **2025 Instructions for Schedule C, "Information on your vehicle", verbatim:**

> *"**If you claim any car and truck expenses, you must provide certain information on the use of your
> vehicle** by completing one of the following. **1.** Complete Schedule C, **Part IV**, if (a) you are
> claiming the standard mileage rate, you lease your vehicle, or your vehicle is fully depreciated;
> and (b) you are not required to file Form 4562 for any other reason… **2.** Complete **Form 4562,
> Part V**, if you are claiming depreciation on your vehicle or you are required to file Form 4562 for
> any other reason."*

🛑 **A figure on line 9 with neither Part IV nor Form 4562 behind it is an incomplete return**, and
🛠️ **ATX will not necessarily stop you** — Part IV is on page 2 of the same schedule and is easy to
scroll past.

| The decision | The two routes, and they are not interchangeable |
|---|---|
| **Standard mileage** | 📎 **2025 rate = `0.70` per business mile**, plus parking and tolls. ⛔ **Then you may NOT also deduct depreciation, rent/lease, or actual operating costs.** 🔑 **So the truck repair and the towing would be INSIDE the mileage rate and disappear from line 9.** ⚠️ **TWO eligibility branches, and the second is easy to miss for an owner-operator:** 📎 *"**Owned** the vehicle and used the standard mileage rate for the **first year** you placed the vehicle in service, **or** **Leased** the vehicle and are using the standard mileage rate for the **entire lease period**."* 🔴 **Neither is established.** ⛔ **And you may NOT use actual expenses on a leased vehicle if you previously used the standard rate on it.** Needs **Schedule C Part IV** |
| **Actual expenses** | 📎 *"Include on line 9 the business portion of expenses for gasoline, oil, **repairs**, **insurance**, license plates, etc."* 🔑 **This is the route the repair and towing figures fit.** ⛔ **Depreciation goes on line 13 and lease payments on line 20a, never on line 9.** Needs **Form 4562 Part V** if depreciation is claimed |

⛔ **THREE THINGS THAT FALL OUT OF THIS AND ARE NOT YET ANSWERED:**

1. 🔴 **WHOSE TRUCK?** §4E already flags it. **If it is the corporation's truck, none of this is his
   Schedule C expense** and line 9 is zero. **That question comes first and the rest is moot without
   it.**
2. 🟠 **Line 9 vs line 21.** **Towing and repair of a VEHICLE he uses in the business belong on line
   9** under actual expenses — **not** on line 21 (*Repairs and maintenance*), which is for the repair
   of other business property. ⛔ **The §4E triage offers "line 9 or line 21" as an open choice; under
   the actual-expense route it is line 9.**
3. 🔴 **VEHICLE INSURANCE IS NOT LINE 15.** The instruction above puts vehicle insurance **inside line
   9**. ⛔ **So if any part of the 26,479 "insurance" is cover on the truck, it does not go to line 15**
   — and the personal car insurance is out altogether.
   ⓘ **And one more the 2025 form adds:** 📎 *"If you use your vehicle for both business and personal
   purposes and you claimed a deduction in 2025 on **Schedule 1-A (Form 1040)** for the vehicle loan
   interest allocable to your personal use, then you **can't** claim a deduction for that same interest
   on Schedule C."* 🔑 **That is the OBBBA car-loan deduction §4E mentions — it and the Schedule C
   cannot both have the same interest.**

#### ⛔ ~~LINE 17 HAS THREE GATES~~ — **MOOT 2026-09-23: THERE IS NOTHING TO DEDUCT**

> ✅ **The client answered that he had NO health insurance in 2025 and paid no bills** (§4G).
> ⛔ **So line 17 is ZERO and none of the three tests below is reached.** 🔑 **Kept because they are
> the right tests for the next client with a real §162(l) deduction** — in particular the employer-plan
> test, which is month-by-month and covers the spouse.
> ⚠️ **And it removes the circularity this section flagged:** with no policy there is no §162(l)/§36B
> interaction to iterate.

| # | The limit | What it does here |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Capped at the earned income from that business** — 📎 **Form 7206 computes it: Schedule C line 31, LESS Schedule 1 line 15 (half the SE tax) and line 16 (self-employed retirement)** | 🟢 **Not binding** — the cap is far larger than 14,867 on either expense branch. ⓘ *An earlier version described it as "Schedule SE line 4a less…"; the computation runs off line 31, not line 4a* |
| 2 | 🔴 **NOT ALLOWED FOR ANY MONTH HE WAS ELIGIBLE FOR AN EMPLOYER PLAN — his OR HIS SPOUSE'S** | ⛔ **This is the one nobody has checked.** 🔑 **It is tested month by month**, and **the spouse's employment is not established in this paper at all.** 📌 **One question: was either of them eligible for subsidised employer cover in any month of 2025?** |
| 3 | 🟠 **The policy must be established under the business** | ⚠️ **A personally-bought individual policy generally qualifies for a sole proprietor** — but the 14,867 arrived as a bare line on a text message with no carrier, no policy, no months |
| ⓘ | **Form 7206** is the computation sheet for this line | 🛠️ **Check whether ATX opens it** — the deduction is not simply typed on Schedule 1 when a computation is required |

🔴 **AND THE CIRCULARITY §4E ITEM 2 FLAGS IS REAL:** if the cover was a **Marketplace** plan with
advance premium credit, §162(l) and §36B feed each other and the IRS gives an iterative method.
⛔ **Decision 6 assumes it was not Marketplace and files to test that** — **the health deduction now
rides on the same unestablished fact, and it did not before.**

### 🔴 4F-iv-a · WHAT THE FILED FORM ACTUALLY SHOWS — corrected 2026-09-24; the first reading was wrong

⛔ **An earlier version of this section said the filed form "carries 200,053 in column (c)". THAT IS
STRUCK.** It was a misreading of the text extract, and it drove a blocking item telling Lilian to
retype a column that is not the problem.

🔑 **How the extract settles it.** The Part II block prints:

```
8 200,053 200,053
9 0
10 0
11 0
```

⚠️ **Lines 9, 10 and 11 print ONE value each — the COMPUTED column (e), 0 − 0 = 0, against blank (c)
and (d).** 🔑 **So each non-empty cell prints one value. Line 8 prints TWO.** ⛔ **If (c) held 200,053
and (d) held 200,053, (e) would compute to 0 and line 8 would print THREE.** ✅ **The self-consistent
reading is (c) BLANK · (d) 200,053 · (e) 200,053.**

📌 **So the filed form already reports what an earlier version of this section told her to change it
TO. Its real defects are elsewhere, and two of them are worse than a wrong column:**

| # | What is wrong on the filed form | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 🔴 **Part I line 1(a) — *Notice of inconsistent treatment* — does NOT appear checked.** The same extraction captures `X` on line **2(a)** *("(a) X S Corporation")*, on Schedule C lines F, G and I, and on Schedule E line 27 — **so a checked box does survive extraction** | ⛔ **Without box 1(a) the form is a notice of nothing.** 📎 The *Caution* names it: *"If you don't notify the IRS that you're reporting an item **(box (a) of Part I, line 1)** inconsistently, any deficiency … **may be assessed immediately**."* 🔑 **The most consequential blank on the form** |
| 2 | 🔴 **Column (b) — NEITHER box appears checked** *(Amount of item · Treatment of item)* | 📎 *"Check both parts of column (b) if either (1) or (2) below applies… **(2) The pass-through entity didn't file a return or give you a Schedule K-1**…"* — not a judgement call when no K-1 arrived |
| 3 | 🟠 **Column (c) is BLANK where the instruction says to enter a figure** | 📎 *"…**enter zero in column (c)**."* ⚠️ **A blank and a zero are not the same, and (e) is defined as (d) less (c)** |
| 4 | 🔴 **Line 4's address, city, state and ZIP are blank; line 6 (the entity's tax year) is blank** | Both fillable from what we hold |
| 5 | 🔴 **No page 2 — Part III, the explanation, is absent entirely** | ⛔ ~~On the two protective forms **Part III is their only content**~~ 🟢 **CORRECTED 2026-09-24 (§4L-ii): all three forms carry line 8 at `0 · 0 · 0`, so Part III is not their only content.** 🔑 **It is still what carries the whole disclosure, and it is MANDATORY on all three — which is the point this row exists to make** |
| 6 | 🔴 **One form, not three** | 📎 *"You must complete and file a separate form for each pass-through entity…"* |

🛑 **Items 1, 2 and 5 — AND THE (c)/(d)/(e) READING ITSELF — must be confirmed ON SCREEN in ATX before
anything is retyped.** ⛔ **A text extract is evidence about the text layer, not the checkbox layer** —
this section has already been wrong once from exactly that, and the correction must not become the
next over-claim.

single `0` on rows 9–11 is a **computed** column (e). 📖 **§3B says the opposite** — *"**8 (e)** …
**Typed, not computed** — it is a fill-in box on the form"*. 🔑 **If (e) is typed, the three zeros are
something else and the count argument needs a different explanation.** ⛔ **The conclusion is still the
best available reading of the extract — two values cannot be three — but it is a READING, and one
glance at the screen settles it for good.**

otherwise dispose of his interest during 2025.

inconsistent with the corporation's return, if a return was filed.

### 🔴 4G-i · THE HOME OFFICE — his answer collides with this file's own residence record

🛑 **Do not act on *"it my home"* without resolving this.** 📖 **The client file records, from Lilian
reading the Form 1098 on 2026-08-12:** he pays **BOTH** — *"the house on it is in **MINNESOTA** and he
lived in **Washington** then **Florida**, so he mortgages one home and rents another."* ✅ **And he
confirmed he had NO rental income in 2025**, so the Minnesota house is not let.

⛔ **A home office has to be in the home where he actually worked.** If he lived in rented housing in
WA and then FL all year, then:

| If the truth is… | Then |
|---|---|
| 🏠 **He worked from the RENTED home (WA/FL)** | 🟠 **The 3,391 rent is the base — 🔴 BUT NOBODY HAS SAID WHETHER IT IS MONTHLY OR ANNUAL**, and §4E's expense table records rent as one of the four items that arrived **with no period stated**. ⚠️ **If it is monthly the base is 40,692, twelve times what this row implies** — ⛔ **establish the period before any line-30 arithmetic.** Then, per Lilian's own email, **mortgage interest, homeowners insurance, property taxes and HOA must all come OUT** |
| 🏡 **He worked from the MINNESOTA house he owns** | ⛔ **Then he was not living where he says he lived**, which moves his **residence**, his **state filings** and the whole §2C-bis analysis. 🔴 **Far bigger than a home office** |
| 🆕 **He bought a home in FL or WA that this file does not know about** | 🔴 **Then there is a property, a mortgage and possibly a second Form 1098 nobody has** |

📌 **ONE QUESTION SETTLES IT, and it is not "do you own or rent":** **"In which house or apartment did

| Part IV line | What it asks | Answer |
|---|---|---|

> miles out of the **personal** total and leaves **44a at 15,634 and line 9 at 10,944 — final.**

> in ATX will flag the wrong one** — and under the wrong one nobody ever revisits line 9.

> $0.70 for each one.**

#### 🔴 LINE 44b — COMMUTING, and the obvious answer is wrong in BOTH directions

⛔ **An earlier version of this section said: *0 if the home office holds; otherwise home-to-first-stop
is commuting and comes out of the 15,634.* 🛑 BOTH HALVES WERE TOO ABSOLUTE.**

📎 **Pub. 463, *No regular place of work*, verbatim:**

> *"If you have **no regular place of work** but ordinarily work in the metropolitan area where you
> live, you **can deduct** daily transportation costs between home and a temporary work site
> **outside** that metropolitan area. … You **can't** deduct daily transportation costs between your
> home and temporary work sites **within** your metropolitan area."*

🛑 **AND THE NEXT BLOCK IS NOT A QUOTATION — an earlier version presented it as one, introduced as
*"the three routes to deductibility, from the same publication"*, and then set out TWO. ⛔ Both the
count and the attribution were wrong.** 🔑 **It is this paper's own paraphrase of the
rule** — the numbered formulation reads like **Rev. Rul. 99-7**'s, which Pub. 463 reflects in prose
rather than reproduces. 🛑 **THAT ATTRIBUTION IS ITSELF UNVERIFIED and must not be repeated as
established** — swapping an unchecked Pub. 463 quote for an unchecked ruling citation would be the
same defect in a better disguise. ⚠️ **Nobody may quote it to a client, to Julia or into a return without
reading the current text first** *(a fetch of `irs.gov/publications/p463` on 2026-09-23 returned only
chapter 1 and did not settle it)*. **The two propositions themselves are corroborated by the
verbatim quotes on either side of this block — the metropolitan-area rule above, and the
"regular places of business" sentence in the table below:**

> **(paraphrase, unverified wording)** — daily transportation costs are deductible where **(1)** he
> has **one or more regular work locations away from his residence**; or **(2)** his **residence is
> his principal place of business**, in which case travel between the residence and another work
> location in the same trade or business is deductible **whether the work is temporary or permanent
> and whatever the distance**.

| The reading | What it does to 44b |
|---|---|
| ⛔ ~~*"No home office → all of it is commuting"*~~ | **FALSE.** He is an interstate operator with no fixed office; **trips to a TEMPORARY site OUTSIDE his metro area stay deductible** with no home office at all. ⚠️ **But the carve-out is for a TEMPORARY site: 📎 *"Daily transportation expenses you incur while traveling from home to one or more REGULAR places of business are generally nondeductible commuting expenses"* — a regular work location commutes at ANY distance.** 🔑 **So within-metro trips reclassify, and so does anything he treats as a regular base — whether he has one is part of the question** |
| ⛔ ~~*"Home office holds → 44b is 0"*~~ | **NOT ENOUGH.** Route (2) needs the residence to be the **PRINCIPAL PLACE OF BUSINESS** — an office qualifying only as a place to meet clients supports **line 30** and still leaves a commute |
| ✅ **What is actually true** | **Each trip is tested.** 📌 **The realistic answer is that 44b is small, not that it is zero or large** — and it is a question for him, not a computation |

📌 **So line 9 is not final, but the downside is far smaller than the earlier wording implied.**

🛑 **AND THE REMOVAL HAS A CONSEQUENCE THAT MUST BE PUT TO HER, BECAUSE AN NOL IS NOT ELECTIVE.**
📎 Instructions for **Form 172**: *"Your carryover is the excess of your NOL deduction over your
**modified taxable income** for the carryback or carryforward year."* 🔑 **The carryover is measured
against the YEAR'S INCOME, not against what was claimed** — so leaving the deduction off the return
does **not** bank it for 2026. ⛔ **If the 537,926 is a valid NOL of his, removing it forgoes the
deduction AND loses the same carryforward.**

📎 **And if it goes back on, it goes on CAPPED.** Instructions for Form 172: *"your NOL deduction …
cannot exceed the sum of: (1) the NOLs carried to the year from tax years beginning before January 1,
2018; plus, (2) the lesser of: (a) the NOLs carried to the year from tax years beginning after
December 31, 2017, or **(b) 80% of the excess (if any) of taxable income computed without regard to
deductions for NOLs, or Qualified Business Income (QBI), or section 250 deductions**…"* The 537,926
arose in **2024** (§2), so all of it is post-2017 and all of it is capped.

| | |
|---|---|
| Taxable income without the NOL and without QBI | 117,673 − 8,313 − 31,500 = **77,860** |
| **80% of it — the allowable deduction** | **62,288** |
| What the filed PDF took | **537,926** |

🔑 **THE THREE OUTCOMES, so the choice is priced:**

⛔ **THE FIRST COLUMN IS NOT A CHOICE — it is what the filed PDF did, and §172(a)(2)(B) bars it.** It
is shown so the movement is visible, **not as an option.** 🔑 **Only the second and third columns are
positions anyone may take.**

| | ⛔ NOL taken in full *(what the PDF did — NOT permissible)* | ✅ NOL capped at 80% | ✅ **NOL removed** *(where it stands now)* |
|---|---|---|---|
| Taxable income | 0 | 12,458 | **62,288** |
| QBI deduction | 0 — **lost** | 3,114 | **15,572** |
| ACTC | 1,700 | 954 | **0** |
| **Total tax** | 16,626 | 16,626 | **21,424** |
| **Balance before penalty** | 14,926 | 15,672 | **21,424** |
| NOL left for 2026 | see Form 172 | ≈ 460,000 – 475,000 | ⚠️ **still consumed by modified taxable income** |

⛔ **This is a question for Lilian, not a conclusion.** Two readings and only she can settle which:
**(a)** the 537,926 is not a valid NOL of his — then removing it is right and §2 needs correcting;
**(b)** it is valid — then it belongs on line 8a at **62,288**. 🛑 **Either way a Form 172 / NOL
statement must be attached showing the carryforward**, and there is **none in the 24-page package**.

   **if** the corrected form ever describes a home he OWNS and worked from, **then** line 37 needs the
   basis, and then the allowed-or-allowable rule bites.

🟡 **And one open item CLOSED by the new source documents:**

✅ **The 15,537 travel figure is sound, and the "5,537" was a dictation error.** 📖 The Double note of
**2026-08-13** already records *"Trip 2378$ - Total trip: 15.537 total expenses"*, and 13,159 + 2,378 =
15,537 exactly. ⛔ **§4G-ii treated this as a live contradiction; it is not.** ⚠️ **What remains is
composition, not amount:** the 13,159 was *"gas, hotels, and food for the year"* — the **gas is inside
the mileage rate**, the **food belongs on line 24b at 50% (or 80% under the DOT rule)**, and **tolls and
parking belong inside line 9**.

✅ **And the home-office figures are sourced after all — every one of them traces to the client's own
sheet.** ⛔ **An earlier reading feared the 1,000 / 100 square feet were the blank template's
