2,232 · property tax 328.83×12 = 3,945.96 · **HOA 449×12 = 5,388 → line 22 "Other"** · utilities 3,204 +
internet 1,524 = **4,728 → line 21** · cleaning 3,444 + repairs 1,752 = **5,196 → line 20** · total
39,093.96 × 10% = **3,909.40** → Schedule C line 30.

### 🛑 4H-iii · AND THAT RECONCILIATION IS THE PROBLEM, NOT THE COMFORT — THE FORM 8829 IS BUILT ON THE WRONG HOUSE

🔴 **The four FIXED indirect expenses on the Form 8829 belong to a house he did not live in** —
mortgage interest, property taxes, HOA and homeowner's insurance. ⛔ **An earlier version said "every
indirect expense", which its own next sentence refutes: utilities and repairs/cleaning are indirect
expenses too and they STAY.** 📖 **The client file has said this since 2026-08-12, from Lilian's own
reading of the Form 1098:**

> *"**The house on the Form 1098 is in MINNESOTA.** He lived in **Washington until May 2025 and Florida
> after**, so **he was paying a mortgage on a house he did not live in, and renting where he did.**
> Nothing was confused: the rent on his figures note and on the Home Office template is real, and so is
> the mortgage."*

🔑 **So the mortgage interest and the property taxes are the MINNESOTA house's costs — those two come
straight off the Form 1098. The HOA and the homeowner's insurance are INFERRED to belong to the same
house**, because a renter pays neither, and ✅ **the return's own Schedule C line E business address is
an APARTMENT in North Miami**, which supports it. ⚠️ **Inferred is not established — say so when you
raise it.** 📌 **The home office was in a RENTED home** — Washington until May, Florida after.

⚖️ **AND THIS IS DELIVERED AS §4G-i's QUESTION, NOT AS A CLOSED FINDING.** 🛑 **The client's own answer
was *"it my home"*** — §4G-i sets out three live branches and one of them is that he worked from the
Minnesota house he owns. ⛔ **A 1098 for Minnesota plus an apartment address is strong, but two records
disagree and** 📖 [`method.md`](../../pre-return-review/method.md) **says show the client both, then
ask.** ✅ **Where this paper lands: the evidence points hard at the rented home, and the question still
goes to him.**

⚠️ **And the client's own sheet shows the same thing without naming it: he left the `Rent Expense` row
BLANK and filled the mortgage row.** He answered the template with the house he pays a mortgage on,
because that is the house he has paperwork for.

📌 **What the corrected Form 8829 runs on is the RENT** — and that is where the **3,391** from his
2026-08-05 list goes, the figure §4E has been carrying as unexplained and unplaced. 🔴 **Its period is
still not stated**, and it is the difference between an annual base of **3,391** and one of **40,692**.

⛔ **Two further consequences, neither of them optional:**

1. **He moved in May, so there were two rented homes — and IF he worked from both, that is two forms.**
   📎 Form 8829's own instruction: *"Use a separate Form 8829 for each home you **used for business**
   during the year."* ⚠️ **The trigger is homes USED FOR BUSINESS, not homes rented** — so this follows
   only once he says he worked from both, which is the same question as above. ⓘ *§4G-i already carries
   the Pub. 587 nuance that the simplified method is available for only one home a year, so the other
   would have to go on actual expenses.*
2. 🟠 **The Minnesota mortgage interest is in the wrong place, and the test it needs will probably
   fail.** A second home's qualified residence interest belongs on **Schedule A** *(the client file
   confirms no 2025 rental income, so it is a residence and not a rental)*. ⛔ **But do the arithmetic
   before calling it recoverable: 17,604 + 3,946 = 21,550 against a 31,500 standard deduction.** Even
   with the state and local taxes added it is short unless something else appears. ✅ **Run the test and
   write the result down** — the point is that it stops being an open question, not that it pays.

🔑 **This also answers §4G-i's "which house did he work in?" — not by choosing between the two he lived
in, but by ruling out the one the form describes.** ⚠️ **Which of the two RENTED homes, and for what
part of the year, is still his to answer.**

## 4J · 🔴 2026-09-23 (fourth pass) — TWO OPEN ITEMS ARE NOW ANSWERED, AND BOTH MOVE THE TAX

🔑 **Both were sitting in §8 as "nobody has read the 2024 document". Both documents were already in
this session.** ⛔ **That is the finding behind the finding:** the paper twice sent a future reader off
to fetch something the firm was already holding, and the honest label for that is not "open" but
"unread". ⓘ *Surfaced while rebuilding the Excel around **what to enter** rather than what the return
shows — a column that cannot be filled with "check this" forces the question to be answered.*

### 4J-i · 💰 THE QBI DEDUCTION IS NOT HIS — the prior-year loss carryforward is **630,737**

📌 **Form 8995 line 3 is filed as `( 0 )`. It should read `630,737`.**

| 📅 **And for next year** | Form 8995 **line 16** becomes **(521,377)** exactly — 109,360 less 630,737. ⚠️ **But that is the figure ON THE RETURN AS IT STANDS, and it moves**: line 2 is Schedule C line 31 less half the SE tax, and §4J's own corrections change both *(the 2,389 on line 16b alone takes it to about (523,600))*. 📌 **Re-read line 16 off the software AFTER the Schedule C is final**, exactly as §7 already says to do for the NOL |

### 4J-ii · 💰 THE ESTIMATED-TAX PENALTY IS NOT OWED AT ALL — the whole **656** comes off

📌 **Enter `0` as the prior-year tax on ATX's Form 2210 input.** Line 8 → 0, line 9 → 0, **Form 1040
line 38 clears to 0**, line 37 falls from **16,330 to 15,674**, and ATX should stop printing a
Form 2210 altogether.

> 🛑 **THIS SECTION SAID `2,120` AND A PENALTY OF `99` FOR ABOUT AN HOUR. BOTH ARE STRUCK, AND THE
> ERROR IS WORTH KEEPING BECAUSE IT IS A TRAP ANYONE WOULD FALL INTO.** ⛔ **Form 2210 line 8 is NOT
> Form 1040 line 24.** The step from *"the 2024 total tax is 2,120"* to *"type 2,120"* is the whole
> mistake, and it was caught in review, not by the pass that made it.

📎 **The instruction, verbatim** *(2025 Instructions for Form 1040, line 38)*:

> *"the **'tax shown on your 2024 return'** is the amount on your 2024 Form 1040 or 1040-SR, line 24,
> **minus the total of any amounts shown on lines 27, 28, and 29**; Schedule 3, lines 9 and 12; and
> Forms 8828, 4137, 5329 (Parts III through IX only), and 8919."*

🧮 **His 2024 return, read line by line:**

| 2024 Form 1040 | | |
|---|---|---|
| line **24** Total tax | **2,120** | Schedule 2 line 21 = Form 5329 **Part I** line 4 — a 10% additional tax on a 21,200 early distribution *(Part I is **not** among the "Parts III through IX" that get subtracted, so it stays in)* |
| line **27** Earned income credit | **(2,250)** | subtracted |
| line **28** Additional child tax credit | **(1,700)** | subtracted |
| line **29** American opportunity credit | — | blank |
| = **TAX SHOWN for 2024** | **(1,830) → ZERO** | it cannot go below zero |

✅ **So the exception applies on its own terms** *(same instruction, "Exception")*: *"You won't owe the
penalty if your 2024 tax return was for a tax year of 12 full months and either of the following
applies. **1. You had no tax shown on your 2024 return** and you were a U.S. citizen or resident for
all of 2024."*

ⓘ **The Form 2210's OWN line 8 instruction is a DIFFERENT computation and reaches the same zero** —
1040 line 22 *(0)* **plus** Schedule 2 line 8 *(2,120, the distributions tax)* **minus** the refundable
credits *(3,950)* = **(1,830) → 0**. ⚠️ **Not "the same thing", as an earlier draft of this section
said** — two different routes that happen to agree, which is stronger than one.

🔍 **AND HERE IS WHY ATX PRODUCED 656 IN THE FIRST PLACE, which is the evidence the mechanism is
right:** the return's own page-28 worksheet shows four required payments of
**3,527 / 3,527 / 3,527 / 3,526 = 14,107** — **exactly line 5** *(90% × 15,674)*. 🔑 **ATX used line 5
because line 8 was empty.** 🧮 *The penalty it then computed reproduces: `3,527 × 7% × 365/303/212/90
days = 247 + 205 + 143 + 61 = 656`, which is what the return prints.*

✅ **AND IT LANDS THE SAME WAY BY THE OTHER ROUTE**, which is what makes it safe: Form 2210 Part I
line 9 is *"the **smaller** of line 5 or line 8"* — line 5 is 90% of this year (**14,107**), line 8 is
the 2024 tax shown (**0**) — so line 9 is **0**, and the form's own routing reads *"Is line 9 more
than line 6? **No. You don't owe a penalty.**"* 🔑 **Both roads give zero, and neither depends on the
other.**

🔵 **THE EARLIER §4D BLOCK WAS RIGHT AND IS RESTORED, NOT SUPERSEDED.** It said *"if the 2024 tax was
zero, the penalty is waived in ALL FIVE branches and this column is moot"*, and named the **two**
things stopping it being settled. ✅ **Both are now closed:**

- ⛔ **The spouse's Schedule C does not produce SE tax.** Her 2024 net profit is **131** *(gross 552
  less 421 of expenses)* — **below the 400 floor** — and **2024 Schedule 2 line 4 is blank**, with no
  Schedule SE filed.
- ⛔ **And the unread page-1 lines cannot rescue it either**, because the test is not *"was there
  income"* but *"was there tax SHOWN"*, and the refundable credits exceed the tax by 1,830.

⚠️ **ONE THING TO CONFIRM RATHER THAN ASSUME**, because the exception has a second limb: **the 2024
return covered 12 full months and they were U.S. residents all year.** 📎 *The Form 8879 states "Tax
Year Ending December 31, 2024", and a 2024 EIC claim itself requires a U.S. main home for more than
half the year — strong corroboration, not proof.* 📌 **One sentence from Lilian closes it.**

⚠️ **DO NOT ADD THE TWO §4J FINDINGS TOGETHER NAIVELY.** 🔑 **This section's "16,330 → 15,674" is the
penalty ALONE; §4J-i pushes about 310 back the other way.** ✅ **Worked together with the vehicle
interest on Schedule C line 16b, the expected figure is **`15,602`** — and the rows still marked
`DECIDE` *(the insurance, the travel split, the meals, the drivers, and above all the Form 8829 rebuilt
on rent)* **will move it again.** 📌 **The workbook's `Read me` carries the whole chain side by side.**

🔑 **AND UNLIKE EVERY OTHER CORRECTION ON THIS RETURN, THIS ONE CAN BE MADE NOW AND LEFT ALONE.**
Line 9 is **0** whatever Schedule C line 16b and Form 8995 line 3 do to this year's tax, because a
smaller-of test with a zero on one side has only one answer.

🛑 **AND DO NOT REACH FOR THE FORM 2210 PART II BOX A "WAIVER".** 📎 *"You request a waiver … of your
**entire** penalty"* — that asks the IRS to **forgive a penalty that IS owed**, is limited to
retirement-after-62 / disability / casualty cases, and **requires an attached statement of reasons**.
⛔ **Here it would be a false claim on a filed return. No penalty is owed in the first place.**

### 4K-v · ⚖️ COLUMN (d) — HER QUESTION: does 200,053 DOUBLE the income? **No. It cannot.**

🗣️ **She asked it precisely** *(2026-09-24)*: the 200,053 is already being taxed through the
Schedule C — SE tax and income tax — so *"lo que tengo miedo es poner ese monto nuevamente en la forma
8082 y duplicar el ingreso"*.

🛑 **THE ANSWER IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A JUDGEMENT CALL: FORM 8082 IS A NOTICE. NOTHING ON IT FLOWS TO ANY
LINE OF THE 1040.** ⛔ **There is no line on the form that carries anywhere, and no instruction to
enter any of its figures on a return.** 🔑 **The income appears ONCE, on Schedule C line 1. Column (d)
does not ADD it — column (d) DESCRIBES what the return already does.**

📎 **What actually settles it is an ABSENCE — there is no instruction anywhere in this form's
instructions carrying a Form 8082 figure to a return** *(the only traffic runs the other way: "enter
the amount as shown on the Schedule K-1")*. ✅ **And the *Who Must File* list corroborates it head-on,
with a case where Form 8082 reports something that never appears on the return at all:**

> *"You believe an item shown on your schedule or statement is incorrect **but it isn't an item that
> otherwise has to be reported on your tax return**. For example, if you believe that the percentage
> shown as your ownership of capital … wasn't properly reported on Schedule K-1, file Form 8082 to
> report this, **even though you aren't otherwise required to report that percentage on your tax
> return**."*

🔑 **A form that can disclose an ownership PERCENTAGE is plainly not an income-reporting form.**

✅ **AND THE SECOND HALF OF HER WORRY RUNS THE OTHER WAY.** She feared it would read as *"money we did
not pay tax on"*. ⛔ **It reads as the opposite** — 🟢 **and §4L-iii answers this the way that survives
the package she chose, so read THAT one.** ⛔ ~~Column (e) = (d) − (c) = **200,053 − 0 = a POSITIVE
200,053**, which says: *the corporation reported nothing to me, and I am reporting 200,053 anyway.*

aggressive one.~~ 🛑 **STRUCK 2026-09-24: under the settled package (d) and (e) are ZERO, so this
arithmetic is void.** 🔑 **§4L-iii's answer does not depend on the figure at all** — the Schedule C is

### 4L-i · 🛑 TWO CORRECTIONS TO §4I-v AND §4K-v — one of them is a plain error

> **She asked why a zero in column (d) would hide the Part III explanation.** ⛔ **IT WOULD NOT, AND
> THIS PAPER NEVER SHOULD HAVE LEFT THAT IMPRESSION.** 🔑 **Part III is MANDATORY whatever line 8 says.**
> ⚠️ *Not "always prints" — this very package proves the software can omit it: §4F-iv-a item 5
> records that page 2 is missing entirely. The mandate is the point; the printing is a
> defect to fix.* 📎 **The instruction says the opposite of the fear** — it is the one place that ties the two
> together, and it makes Part III MORE important when line 8 is empty, not less:
>
> > *"You **don't need to complete lines 8 through 11** if not reporting a change to the amount or
> > treatment of a monetary item; **however, you must include an explanation of the change(s) in
> > Part III**."*
>
> ⓘ *What this paper actually said was that the **protection** would not reach the figure. That is a
> different claim, it was not what she read, and — see below — it was also wrong.*

🛑 **AND THE PROTECTION ARGUMENT ITSELF WAS OVER-STATED. STRUCK.** 📎 **The Caution, read closely:**

> *"If you **don't notify the IRS** that you're reporting an item **(box (a) of Part I, line 1)**
> inconsistently, any deficiency … may be assessed immediately."*

🔑 **The notice is given by TICKING BOX 1(a) and filing the form with its description and its
explanation. It is not given by a figure in column (d).** ⛔ **So "keep 200,053 or the disclosure does
not cover it" was wrong: the disclosure is the WHOLE FORM**, and the 200,053 is named in column (a)
and again in Part III whatever column (d) says.

✅ **WHAT ACTUALLY GOVERNS COLUMNS (c), (d) AND (e) IS SIMPLER, AND IT IS THE REAL RULE:**

> 📌 **They must describe the SAME ITEM that column (a) describes.** 📎 *Column (d) is "the amount
> you're reporting **as the correct amount**"* — the correct amount **of that item**.

| If column (a) describes… | then (c) · (d) · (e) are… | and Part III explains… |
|---|---|---|
| **the FUNDS he received** | `0` · `200,053` · `200,053` | why they are on Schedule C |
| **his SHAREHOLDER'S SHARE under §1366** | `0` · `0` · `0` | that he reports none of it, **and** that the funds went on Schedule C |

⛔ **What is NOT allowed is mixing them** — describing the funds in column (a) and then writing 0 in
column (d), or the reverse.

### 4L-ii · ✅ HER INSTINCT PRODUCES THE MORE COHERENT PACKAGE — and this paper now recommends it

🗣️ **She wants `0` in column (d) on the two silent corporations, "porque en realidad el cliente cree
que es cero", with Part III carrying everything we know.** ✅ **That is right, and carrying the same
description across all three forms is BETTER than what §4I-v recommended**, for one reason this paper
had not weighed: **three forms filed on the same day about three corporations in the same dispute
should describe the same KIND of item the same way.** ⛔ **Describing the item as "funds" on one and as
"shareholder's share" on the other two invites the question of why they differ.**

📌 **RECOMMENDED, all three forms:**

| Field | All three |
|---|---|
| **1(a)** notice of inconsistent treatment | ✅ **tick** |
| **2(a)** S Corporation | ✅ **tick** |
| **8 column (a)** | `Shareholder's share of S corporation income, deductions and credits; no Schedule K-1 received.` |
| **8 column (b)** | ✅ **BOTH boxes** |
| **8 (c) · (d) · (e)** | `0` · `0` · `0` |
| **Part III** | the texts below — **and the Midwest one names the 200,053 and the Schedule C** |

✅ **AND LINE 8 IS COMPLETED — the instructions do reach it, and §4F-iv holds.** 📎 The Note at the head
of the *Specific Instructions* is written for exactly these facts: *"If the pass-through entity didn't
file a return or give you a Schedule K-1 … **by the time you're required to file your return, complete
Parts I and II to the best of your knowledge**."* 🔑 **Line 8 is Part II.** ⚠️ **The general waiver
below it — *"You don't need to complete lines 8 through 11 if not reporting a change…"* — is the broad
rule, and it bows to the Note addressed to this fact pattern** *(§4F-iv, and the reasoning was corrected
there on 2026-09-24: the conclusion was right and had been hung on the wrong Note)*.

🔑 **BUT THAT SAME WAIVER SENTENCE IS THE PROOF OF WHAT §4L-i ANSWERS, AND IT IS WHY IT IS QUOTED HERE
AT ALL:** *"…**however, you must include an explanation of the change(s) in Part III**."* ⛔ **Part III
is mandatory even in the case where line 8 is entirely empty — so a zero in column (d) cannot possibly
hide it.** ⚠️ **That argument does not depend on line 8 being optional, and an earlier version of this
paragraph wrongly concluded from it that it was.**

⌨️ **IF ATX'S COLUMN (a) FIELD IS TOO SHORT** *(it is one line on the screen, and the full sentence
above is 94 characters)* — **type this instead, on all three:**

```
Shareholder's share of S corp income; no K-1 received.
```

⛔ **Do not shorten it any further than that** — the two facts it must carry are **what the item is**
*(the shareholder's share, not a payment)* and **why it is being disclosed** *(no K-1 arrived)*. ✅ **The
full sentence is always preferred if it fits; Part III carries the rest either way.**

🔑 **The 200,053 is still fully disclosed** — it is in Midwest's Part III, in plain words, with where
it was reported. ⛔ **Nothing is hidden by a zero, and nothing is duplicated.**
⚠️ **§4I-v's Package B remains defensible** *(funds in column (a), 200,053 in (d))* **and is still
hers to take — but then Midwest's column (a) must be the funds description, not the one above.**

### 4L-iii · 💬 HER FEAR, ANSWERED A THIRD WAY BECAUSE THE FIRST TWO DID NOT LAND

🗣️ *"Tengo miedo de que reportar esos $200,000 en la forma 8082 le diga al IRS que no he pagado
impuestos por ese dinero, y sin embargo lo incluí en el Sch C para pagarlos."*

🔑 **The two forms do two different jobs, and only one of them is about tax:**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Schedule C** | ⚖️ **This is where the tax is paid.** The 200,053 is on line 1, it flows to line 31, to Schedule 1, to Schedule SE, and it produces **16,289 of self-employment tax and the income tax on top.** |
| **Form 8082** | 📄 **This is a letter, not a computation.** It says: *the corporation never sent me a Schedule K-1, so here is what I did instead.* |

⛔ **Form 8082 has NO tax line, no total, and no instruction anywhere telling you to carry a figure
from it to the Form 1040.** 🔑 **If the IRS were meant to tax anything off this form, there would have
to be a line saying "enter this on Form 1040, line …". There is not one — not for column (d), not for
column (e), not anywhere on the form.**

✅ **And the reading she fears is the reverse of how it lands.** ⛔ **Filing NOTHING is what would look
like undeclared money**: a shareholder of three S corporations, no K-1s on file with the IRS, and a
Schedule C appearing from nowhere. ✅ **Filing the three forms says: *I know I am a shareholder, I know
no K-1 came, I am telling you what I received and where I put it.*** 🔑 **It is the taxpayer raising
his own hand.**

### 4L-iv · ⚠️ HER SHARPEST POINT — and it is the reason these two forms are worth filing

🗣️ *"No sabemos si las compañías generaron ganancias y tal vez esas ganancias no fueron entregadas al
cliente, pero aun así existen."*

✅ **That is exactly right, and it is the §1366 problem: an S-corporation shareholder is taxed on his
share whether or not a single dollar is distributed.** ⛔ **So "he took no money" does NOT establish
that he owes no tax on Truck Leasing or AVDM** — it establishes only what he knows.

🔑 **Which is precisely what a Form 8082 is for.** 📌 **Reporting zero with a full Part III says: *I am
reporting nothing because nothing was furnished to me and I have no way to find out.*** ✅ **If a
Schedule K-1 surfaces later showing income, the return already carries his disclosure, made before
anyone asked** — and the Part III text below commits him to **acting** on it when it arrives. ⛔ **NOT to agreeing with it** — see the second strike under the texts: promising in advance to report whatever the K-1 says would surrender the very right this form preserves.
ⓘ *Context, not evidence about 2025: both were tiny losses in 2024, and there are **two different
figures**, which is what makes this easy to garble.* 🔑 **(168) each IS a Schedule E figure** — rows B
and C of Part II, the nonpassive-loss column, and the arithmetic proves it: `630,737 + 168 + 168 =
631,073`, which is Schedule E line 32 and Schedule 1 line 5. *(It appears on the 199A/QBI worksheet
too, at the same amount — the same loss seen by two forms.)* ⚠️ **(138) each is something else
entirely** — prior-years' **unallowed passive** loss on **Form 8582**, totalling the 276 of §2C.
⛔ ~~An intermediate version of this line said the 168 is a QBI figure and "not a Schedule E figure".
That was an over-correction and is withdrawn~~ — ⚠️ *it would have contradicted §2A and §5A's own
tie-out check, both of which have carried the 168 as Schedule E all along.* **None of it says anything
about what 2025 did.*

| **The text blocks below** | ⛔ **They carry NO `8`** — on the assumption that ATX supplies it from its own column, where pasting it into the prose as well would print it twice |

**FORM 8082 — MIDWEST EXPEDITED CORP, EIN 47-5147826** *(updated 2026-09-24 for the zero package —
the last paragraph of the middle block is new, and it is what makes the text agree with a column (d)
of zero)*

```
Schedule K-1 not received.

The taxpayer was a shareholder of MIDWEST EXPEDITED CORP, EIN 47-5147826, during 2025.
He performed services for the corporation during the year and received 200,053 dollars
from it. During 2025 he was removed from all access to the
corporation's books, records and accounts following a dispute with the other
shareholder, and he has been unable to obtain any information from the corporation
since.

No Form W-2 and no Schedule K-1 for 2025 were issued to him, and he has no way to
determine whether the corporation filed a return for the year.

Because no Schedule K-1 was received by the due date of this return including
extensions, the 200,053 dollars he received is reported as gross receipts on
Schedule C, and the expenses he paid personally in connection with that work are
deducted on that Schedule C. No amount from this corporation is reported on
Schedule E.

The amounts shown in columns (c) and (d) are zero. Column (c) is zero because the
corporation reported nothing to him. Column (d) is zero because no amount from this
corporation is reported as a pass-through item on his return: the 200,053 dollars is
reported on Schedule C as described above, and not as a distributive share.

The taxpayer remains a shareholder of the corporation. He did not sell, transfer or
otherwise dispose of his interest during 2025.

This form is filed to give notice that the taxpayer's treatment of this item may be
inconsistent with the corporation's return, if a return was filed.
```

## 6 · ⚠️ Limits of the 2024 read — state these before relying on anything above

- **4 of 44 pages extracted only partially** (14, 15, 25, 44). Identified: the spouse's Schedule C
  header, a phone-expense line, and two name lists. ⛔ **None is a Form 7203** — but an absence
  established from those four pages alone would not be evidence.
  🔴 **AND THIS IS NOT ABSTRACT: the Schedule C header is on one of them, and its attachment
  sequence (09) is MISSING from the sequence scan §2E used.** **A form that is in the package was
  invisible to that method.** ⛔ **Never rest a "the form is not here" claim on the sequence scan
  alone.**
- 🔵 **THE 36-PAGE 1120-S READ (§2F) HAS ITS OWN BOUND, AND §2F LIVES OR DIES ON IT.** Almost every
  load-bearing claim in §2F is a **blank** — Schedule L line 19, K-1 item I, paid-in capital,
  salaries and wages, M-2 distributions, K-1 box 16. **1 of 36 pages extracted only partially: page
  35**, which is an **Illinois §179 gain/recapture summary** — ✅ **neither Schedule L (page 6) nor
  Schedule M-2 (page 7) is affected**, and both are independently corroborated by the two roll-forward
  ties in §5. ⚠️ **But the lesson from the 44-page read stands: a form on a partial page can be
  invisible to a scan.**
  ⚠️ **And one blank is doing more work than it should: K-1 box 16 carries codes A-E.** Reading it as
  *"0"* asserts **no nondeductible expenses (code C)** for a 7.6M trucking company, which is
  improbable — **a live alternative is that box 16's codes did not extract.** ⛔ **So "no
  distributions in 2024" rests properly on M-2 line 7 being blank; box 16 corroborates it rather
  than proving it.**
- ⚠️ **1040 page 1's income lines extracted poorly.** Lines 2b, 3b, 4b, 6b and **7 (capital gains)**
  were never read; the W-2 figure in §2B is derived on the assumption they are zero. **1040 line 7
  is Form 461 line 3**, so this is a real gap in §2E, not housekeeping.
- **10 undecodable glyph tokens** were masked. An absence near one proves nothing.
- The redactor masked **22 SSN/ITIN · 1 labelled account/routing · 3 bare 9+ digit runs · 12 street
  lines**; **8 EINs were kept** (public). Names are not masked.
- **Only tax year 2024 was read**, which is the whole of the standing permission. If an earlier NOL
  exists and was simply never entered on the 2024 return, that is a finding to raise — **not a
  licence to open 2023.**

> ⛔ **THESE THREE ARE ADDITIONAL TO THE FULL BRIEFING, NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR IT.** The procedure is
> [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) **§4C** — reason before finding, and
> every item labelled a **DECISION**, an **OPEN ITEM** or a **DEFECT**. ⚠️ **At least four things
> Julia is owed are NOT in this section:** §4B's undecided line-6 variant *(which sets the 2026

### ① No 2024 Form 7203 → **we do not know the client's basis**

**Her point:** *"el hecho de que no hubiera Forma 7203 en el año pasado hace que no sepamos el basis
del cliente."*

⚠️ **This is TWO items, and an earlier draft merged them into "an open item closed by a decision" —
which is not one of the three labels and let a live question hide behind a settled one.**

| 🟢 **DECISION — confirm or overturn** | |
|---|---|
| **What was decided** | **Lilian, 2026-09-22: the prior return is ACCEPTED, not audited** (§2F-i), which sets opening 2025 stock basis at **ZERO** |
| **The chain** | The 2024 return deducted the loss **in full** and suspended nothing → **§1366(d)(1)** caps a loss at stock plus debt basis, **so the return asserts basis of at least the loss** → **§1367** then reduces basis by the losses deducted, *"but not below zero"* |
| ⚠️ **Its stated limit** | **Accepting the return proves basis was *at least* the loss. It does NOT prove it was not MORE** (§2F-i). Zero is used because nothing in the two returns read evidences more, and **he has never been asked** |
| ⛔ **Who has not signed off** | **Julia.** Lilian directed the work; §4 decision 4 records that the signer has not confirmed it |

| 🟠 **OPEN ITEM — still hers to answer** | |
|---|---|
| **The missing FORMS, which the ruling does not reach** | **Three Forms 7203 were required, one per corporation, and none appears in the text extracted from the 44-page return** *(§2D — a bounded read with partial pages; §2E proved that method can miss a form on one)*. **Form 461 was owed too** *(§2F-i and §8 item 0 — the "$152,500 on any one line" trigger)*. 🔑 **Her ruling accepts the FIGURES; it says nothing about forms that were legally required and not filed** (§8 item 1) |

📌 **AND THE MAGNITUDE GOES WITH IT — this is the part the briefing exists to carry.** Lilian's own
escalation clause is *"a menos que sea un **error extremadamente grande**, [Julia] tendrá que
decidir"*, so the figures are the trigger, not colour: **visible basis of ≈ 60,750 against a 630,737
deduction — a gap of roughly 570,000, about 90%** *(figures from §2F-i — **re-derive there if either
end moves**; they also appear in §8 0A, and this is the third home)*. ✅ **The answer given to her test: LARGE IN SIZE,
NOT ESTABLISHED AS AN ERROR**, because a corporation's return cannot measure a shareholder's basis at
all. ⛔ **It does not stop the work; it is owed before the return is filed** (§8 0A).

### ② 🟠 **OPEN ITEM** — no 2025 company return in hand and no 2025 K-1 → **we do not know what the money IS**

**Her point:** *"no sabemos si esos $200,000 que recibió el cliente son capital gains o si fueron
ganancias que en realidad generó la compañía — lo que hizo aumentar el basis del cliente y que, al
recibirlos, no fue una ganancia de capital."*

🔑 **That is exactly §1367's ordering, and it is why the reporting choice matters.** ⚠️ **But it is
THREE branches, not two — an earlier draft wrote it as a clean either/or, which is the same defect
§4B item 2 and §4D ⑥ have each caught in this paper before:**

| Branch | What the money is | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **The corporation EARNED it** | his allocated share | ✅ The income raises basis **first**, the cash then comes out against it — **no capital gain** |
| **It earned nothing** | a distribution against **zero** basis | 🔴 **§1368(b)(2) capital gain** — Form 8949 → Schedule D → Form 1040 line 7a |
| 🔴 **PART OR ALL WAS A LOAN DRAW** | ⛔ **neither income nor a distribution** | **§2F-iii's fifth character, §8 item 0B.** 🔑 **§4D ⑥ calls it "the one reading under which this return reports the wrong thing entirely rather than the right thing imprecisely" — and nobody can currently rule it out** |

⚠️ **A fourth state is live and §9A ④ names it: PART SALARY, PART DISTRIBUTION** (§4D ⑤).
📌 **All of them, with where each figure lands, are §4D — which works FIVE scenarios plus the loan
draw, not four.** ⓘ *We do not HOLD the 2025 1120-S; whether the corporation filed one is itself
unknown (§3B line 5), and §4C notes a W-2 would make it more likely to exist than this file assumes.*

### ③ 🟢 **DECISION taken on an ASSUMPTION** — and both halves get said

**Her point:** *"no estamos seguros de si la compañía tuvo ganancias y si todo o parte de este dinero
está excluido de capital gains. Eso es una nota, una suposición que vamos a hacer, y por eso vamos a
incluirlo en la casilla 1 del K1 input."*

| | |
|---|---|
| **The assumption** | **That the corporation earned at least this much, and that the money is his allocated share.** ⛔ **Nothing establishes it.** It is why the figure goes in **box 1 of the K-1 input** rather than being left out |
| **What it buys** | The income raises basis **before** the distribution reduces it, so **no §1368(b)(2) gain arises** — ⚠️ **CONDITIONALLY.** It holds only while **2025 is an income year** *(the firm's chosen reporting figure, not a fact)* and only while **debt basis really is zero** *(a reading of a balance sheet at two dates — if he did lend, the restoration goes to the DEBT first and the distribution becomes taxable)*. 🔑 **The 2025 K-1 settles both** (§4B item 1, §8 item 2) |
| **What it costs** | ⚠️ **The figure is CASH and box 1 is an ALLOCATED SHARE** — §8 0a records it as a known overstatement |
| **What is disclosed, precisely** | **Form 8082 discloses THIS item and only this one:** that no K-1 was furnished and that the box-1 amount is the taxpayer's own record and an estimate (§3B). ⛔ **It does NOT disclose ①'s basis uncertainty** *(a prior-year matter on another firm's return, which Form 8082 does not reach)* **and it does NOT disclose the payroll question** *(the optional shareholder-officer paragraph that would give the context is drafted, unadopted, and Julia's — §3B, §4F-iv)* |

### ④ 🛑 **PRE-FILING BLOCKER — its own item, because it is not a decision and must not sit inside one**

**Her point:** *"tampoco sabemos si algo de este dinero se corrió como nómina o si todos son
distribuciones."*

⛔ **This is not an open question carried into the return. It has to be answered BEFORE filing.**
🔑 **§4C route 5:** if a W-2 was filed with the SSA and never furnished to him, **Form 1040 line 1a of
zero is matched against it automatically** — **that does not make the return visible, it makes it
WRONG.** ✅ **And it is answerable without his memory**: the IRS wage & income transcript *(Form 8821, and 2025
data has posted since February)*, his bank descriptions, the regularity and netness of the deposits,
and his `ssa.gov` record. 📩 **Three questions drafted in §4C.**
🛑 **BUT THE TRANSCRIPT ONLY ANSWERS IT ONE WAY, AND THIS ITEM GATES TRANSMISSION: a HIT settles it; a
BLANK does NOT.** A blank shows only that **no W-2 was FILED with the IRS** — ⛔ **not that no payroll
was run**, which is consistent with **§4C outcome ②: payroll ran and no W-2 was ever filed**, the
branch Form 4852 exists for. ⚠️ **So a blank transcript does not clear this item on its own; it has to
be read with the bank descriptions and his `ssa.gov` record.**

### ⛔ ~~THE ONE-LINE VERSION~~ — **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23, kept so the change is visible**

> ~~**We do not know his basis, we do not know what the money is, and we are reporting it as box-1 income
> on an assumption — which keeps the distribution from becoming a capital gain *provided* 2025 is an
> income year and debt basis really is zero. Form 8082 discloses the estimated figure; it does not
> disclose the other two. And the payroll question is answered before we file, not after.**~~

### 🔑 THE ONE-LINE VERSION, REWRITTEN FOR THE SCHEDULE C ROUTE

**We do not know his basis and we are not going to find out this year — no Form 7203 is filed either,
so it defers to 2026 with one more blank year behind it. We do not know what the money is, and your
route reports it as self-employment income, which is the one character the Schedule SE instructions
say it cannot have if he was performing services as an employee. That choice costs roughly
17,000–27,000 in SE tax the NOL cannot touch — a band, not a figure, and its floor rises as expenses
come out (§4E) — and Form 8919 would have charged about 13,800, so it
should be a trade you priced, not a default. Three Forms 8082 disclose that no K-1 was received from any
of the three corporations. All three report zero in Part II, because what we are declining to report is
his share of each corporation's income, and we report none of it; the 200,053 is named in the Midwest
form's Part III, in plain words, with the Schedule C it went on. The notice is given by ticking box 1(a)
and filing the form — not by a figure — so nothing is hidden by the zeros. Lilian chose that package on
24 September; the alternative, describing the item as the funds received and putting 200,053 in
column (d), is set out in §4F-iv-b and is yours to take instead.
And the payroll question is answered before we file, not after — because under this route
a W-2 in the transcript contradicts the Schedule C, not just a blank line.**

📌 **§4E carries the figures and the quotations behind every clause of that paragraph — ⚠️ **except the Form 8082 clause, which rests on the §4F-iv-b banner (settled 2026-09-24), §4L-i and §4L-ii.****

### 4M-iii · 🔴 DEFECT 1 — THE NOL IS 1,777 TOO HIGH, and it is our arithmetic that went stale

📖 **Schedule 1 line 8a reads `(62,288)`. The 80% cap on THIS return is `60,511`.**
📎 **§172(a)(2)(B):** 80% of taxable income figured **without the NOL, without QBI, without §250.**
🔑 **The return's own Form 8995 line 2 gives that base:**

```
QBI / taxable income before the NOL   107,139      (115,284 − 8,145)
less the standard deduction          (31,500)
                                    ─────────
                                      75,639   × 80%  =  60,511
```

**superseded** Schedule C — before the car-loan interest moved into line 9 and dropped the profit to
115,284. 🛑 **ATX did not recompute the cap when the profit fell, and neither did this workbook.**

### 4M-vi · 🛠️ THE REDACTOR REFUSED THE FIRST READ, AND THE PATTERN WAS THE BUG

⛔ **`redact.py` exited 4:** *"1 identifier-shaped run(s) survived redaction … NNN\n NN       NNNN"*.
🔑 **Inspected by hand with every digit blanked: it was Schedule 8812 Part II-B** — an amount ending one
line, then the next line's **number gutter** and the literal `1040` of *"1040 and 1040-SR filers"*.
⛔ **Nothing was wrong with the document.** ✅ **`SSN_LOOSE`'s separator class was `[-\s.]`, which spans a
NEWLINE — and three digit groups on two different visual lines of a layout extraction are, by
construction, different values.** 📌 **Narrowed to `[-\t .]`**, with cross-line candidates **counted and
reported** so the narrowing is never silent. ⚠️ **A wide-spaced SSN on ONE line is still caught** — tested.
